Every word of law in this repository comes from these sources. Lexifina reads them and records each act's dated versions, and the laws that made each change, in one corpus file. This repository is built from that file.

| Commits | Kind |
| ---: | --- |
| 260 | first version of an act |
| 2088 | one law on its in-force day |
| 3 | changes no amendment note explains, credited to the publisher |

Other counts:
- 5 commits on the branch `futuro`: laws not yet in force on 2026-10-31.
- 38 commits dated 1 January 1970: in force before 1970, with the real day in their `In-force` trailer.
- 2192 laws in the record.
- 5283 tags.

## Dates

Each commit is dated at noon (America/Bogota) on the day the change took effect. The other dates, enacted and published, are trailers.

When a law gives no date of its own, the default rule applies: Ley 4 de 1913 (Código de Régimen Político y Municipal), arts. 52 and 53: a law binds two months after its promulgation unless it fixes its own day. Actos Legislativos (amendments to the Constitution) state their own vigencia, almost always 'rige a partir de su publicación'. http://www.secretariasenado.gov.co/senado/basedoc/ley_0004_1913.html

## How the history is built

`tools/build.py` turns the corpus into this history in one pass: one commit per law, on the day it took effect. The same corpus always gives the same commit hashes. `tools/check_repo.py` reads every version back from git and compares it with the corpus. `tools/on.py` shows the law as it stood on any date. The corpus format is described in `tools/SCHEMA.md`.

- Corpus: sha256 `615f7e1d05d933194d36d8ab6543ca9a468a735c8240466add08f3f598377070` (79547827 bytes).
- Builder blob hashes (`git hash-object`): `build.py` `a4faa25af85facb64b956b372f6b528aafdd7ec1`, `check.py` `3d00541d258745461ab52b1b0cb4e1716a27a95d`, `on.py` `644a6c0857fbcc72562032b58d57aa113fee0641`.
- Python 3.12.10; git version 2.46.0.windows.1.

## Checks

3097 versions of 260 acts read back from git on the day each took effect and on the day before: 0 mismatches. Trailers: 0 commits whose trailers git cannot parse. Health: 0 merge commits on main, 0 commit dates that go backwards, 0 commits dated after the build.

No second build of this corpus has been compared yet.

## Decisions made by hand

- Source: the Secretaría General del Senado's basedoc, the only free official-site source that keeps each provision's earlier texts ('Legislación Anterior'). SUIN-Juriscol (Ministerio de Justicia) does not give earlier texts.
- Versions are rebuilt per provision from the source's earlier texts ('Texto original de la Constitución Política:', 'Texto modificado por el Acto Legislativo N de AAAA:'), its inline amendment markers ('<Inciso adicionado por el artículo 1 del Acto Legislativo 1 de 2005 ...>') and its Notas de Vigencia. A part marked 'adicionado' did not exist before the act that added it.
- In-force days: an Acto Legislativo takes effect on the day of its publication in the Diario Oficial, as the source's notes state ('Rige a partir de su publicación'), unless the note quotes another vigencia clause: Acto Legislativo 1 de 2001 from 1 January 2002, Actos Legislativos 1 and 4 de 2007 from 1 January 2008, and the election-day clauses of Actos Legislativos 2 de 2005 (12 March 2006), 3 de 2005 (14 March 2010) and 3 de 2007 (28 October 2007).
- Election days are taken from Registraduría Nacional del Estado Civil pages, not written by hand: 12 March 2006 from 'Calendario Electoral 2006 - 2010' (Resolución 0497 de 2005), https://www.registraduria.gov.co/Calendario-Electoral-2006-2010.html; 14 March 2010 from Comunicado de Prensa No. 050 de 2009, https://web.archive.org/web/20110726040247id_/http://www.registraduria.gov.co/ESTA-LISTO-EL-PRE-CALENDARIO.html; 28 October 2007 from the Registraduría home page as captured on 30 September 2007 ('el proceso electoral del 28 de octubre'; the year is the capture's), https://web.archive.org/web/20070930160642id_/http://www.registraduria.gov.co/. All three agree with the days used before.
- Acto Legislativo 3 de 2002 says it 'rige a partir de su aprobación' and applies gradually from 2005; its text changes are dated from its publication (20 December 2002).
- Constitutional Court rulings that struck text (INEXEQUIBLE) are laws of kind 'court', dated by the day of the ruling as the source gives it (rulings take effect from their date unless the Court says otherwise, Ley 270 de 1996 art. 45). When a whole Acto Legislativo was struck, the provision returns to its text before that act. Struck words shown in the current text are in force until the ruling and removed from it.
- Rulings that upheld text on a condition of interpretation (exequibilidad condicionada) change no words: they are court laws with a note, credited to the provision on the ruling's day. Plain EXEQUIBLE rulings, inhibitions and 'estarse a lo resuelto' rulings are not recorded.
- The first version is the 1991 Constitution as the source gives it: the second corrected edition (Gaceta Constitucional No. 116 of 20 July 1991), dated from promulgation on 4 July 1991 (art. 380). The 1991 Aclaración published in Gaceta Constitucional No. 125 is its own commit, kind 'decree'.
- Where the source names an act that changed a provision but does not give the text in force after it (a later act changed the same words again), that act and the next one with a known text are credited together on the later day.
- Avance Jurídico's editorial notes and markers (<...>) are not part of the text. The article heading is written as '# Art. N'; the heading of books, titles and chapters are kept in titulos.md, a heading shown while an article under it exists.
- Placeholder line: where the source names the act that changed a provision but gives no text in force after it (or no 1991 text), the provision reads '[La fuente no da el texto vigente en esta fecha.]' for that period, credited to the act named. Cases: art-122 from 1991-07-04, art-122 from 1991-09-25, art-122 from 2004-01-08, art-256 from 2015-07-01, art-263 from 1991-07-04, art-322 from 1991-07-04, art-328 from 2007-07-06, art-361 from 2011-07-18.
- Approximations, from the source's own notes: 241: struck text but no dated ruling; struck text dropped from all versions; 250: slot chain ends on a text other than today's; today's text used from 2011-11-24; 328: al-2-2017 marked INEXEQUIBLE but no dated ruling found; TRANSITORIO ACL02021-5: slot chain ends on a text other than today's; today's text used from 2021-08-25.
- Headings (titulos.md): the publisher's page links 'Anterior | Siguiente' that the basedoc prints after the last heading of a page are not words of that heading and are left out.
- Código Civil and Ley 1581 de 2012: source is the Secretaría General del Senado's basedoc, the only free official source that keeps each article's earlier texts ('Legislación Anterior').
- Código Civil: versions are rebuilt per article from the source's earlier texts ('Texto original del Código Civil:', 'Texto modificado por la Ley 1a. de 1976:'), its inline amendment markers ('<Artículo modificado por el artículo 1o. del Decreto 2820 de 1974 ...>') and its Notas de Vigencia. A part marked 'adicionado' did not exist before the act that added it.
- Código Civil: the first version is Ley 84 de 1873 (the code of the United States of Colombia), dated from its sanction on 26 May 1873 because the source gives no other day; Ley 57 de 1887 adopted it for the Republic from 90 days after its own publication (19 July 1887), with the reforms it made.
- Código Civil: derogations the source calls tacit ('derogado tácitamente', 'orgánicamente') change no text and are not recorded; only express changes are.
- Código Civil: the notes cite the Código de Procedimiento Civil and the Código Sustantivo del Trabajo by name; they are Decreto 1400 de 1970 and Decreto 2663 de 1950.
- In-force days of amending acts: the act's own vigencia clause as its basedoc page gives it, or as the Código Civil page quotes it; a day that a provision's own note gives ('La derogatoria rige a partir del 1o. de enero de 2014') applies to that provision. Acts the basedoc does not hold (most acts before 1992) are dated by the default rule: before 1913, from publication in the Diario Oficial (Código Civil arts. 11 and 12); from 1913, two months after it (Ley 4 de 1913, art. 52). The list below gives the day and the rule for each act.
- Typing slips in the source, corrected: 'Ley 25 de 1892' (its note gives the Diario Oficial of 18 December 1992) is Ley 25 de 1992; 'Ley 2820 de 1974' is Decreto 2820 de 1974; 'Ley 95 de 1980' in art. 217's earlier text is Ley 95 de 1890 (the reform of arts. 214-217 that Ley 1060 de 2006 later repealed as 'los artículos 5o y 6o de la Ley 95 de 1890'); the Colpensiones heading of Ley 8 de 1922 dates its Diario Oficial No. 18.199bis to 23 February 1913, read as 1922.
- Old acts the basedoc does not hold are read, for their heading and vigencia clause only, from the same Avance Jurídico compilation as published by Colpensiones (normativa.colpensiones.gov.co).
- Where the source shows words struck as INEXEQUIBLE but names no dated ruling for them (arts. 669, 1119, 1527), the struck words are left out of every version; art. 312's struck text is likewise left out.
- Today's text on the source page is taken as the text in force now; where an earlier-text box gives the same act's wording slightly differently (art. 200), the page's current text wins. Articles the source shows with no current text because it calls them derogated tacitly or 'orgánicamente' leave on the in-force day of the act it names: art. 50 from Ley 5 de 1975 (1975-01-28, the first of Ley 5 de 1975, the Código del Menor and the Código de la Infancia y la Adolescencia, as Sentencia C-336 de 2016 analyses), arts. 196 and 208 from Ley 28 de 1932 (1933-01-19; Sentencia C-379 de 1998), art. 636 from Decreto 2150 de 1995, arts. 40, 42 and 43 (1995-12-06; Sentencia C-670 de 2005).
- The last version of every article of both acts is checked against the source page's current text (struck words removed, publisher markers and bracketed epigraphs dropped): all match.
- Constitutional Court rulings that struck text (INEXEQUIBLE) are laws of kind 'court', dated by the day of the ruling as the source gives it. Rulings that upheld text on a condition of interpretation change no words: they are court laws with a note, credited to the article on the ruling's day. Plain EXEQUIBLE rulings and inhibitions are not recorded.
- Where the source names an act that changed an article but does not give the text in force after it, the article's text for that period is the line '[La fuente no da el texto vigente en esta fecha.]'.
- Avance Jurídico's editorial notes, markers (<...>) and article epigraphs in angle brackets are not part of the text. The headings of books, titles and chapters are kept in titulos.md, a heading shown while an article under it exists.
- Ley 1581 de 2012: one text since promulgation on 18 October 2012 (no amendment to its words); the Constitutional Court's prior review of the bill (C-748 de 2011) predates it. Decreto Legislativo 538 de 2020 art. 8 is recorded as an event that changed no words.

- Headings (titulos.md): the publisher's page links 'Anterior | Siguiente' that the basedoc prints after the last heading of a page are not words of that heading and are left out.
- Signature blocks at the end of the last article ('El Presidente del Honorable Senado de la República, ...', 'La Presidenta del honorable Senado ...', 'Publíquese y cúmplase. Dado en ...') are not part of the text and are cut: ley-1581-2012 art-0030.
- Código Civil: article words the page puts under anchors named like headings ('Nivel001', 'TITULO I', ...) had been read as headings, so they were missing from their articles (in titulos.md, or nowhere); they are back in them: art. 642 (its heading in the text, under an anchor named 'Nivel001').
- Código Penal, Código General del Proceso: versions are rebuilt per article from the basedoc's earlier texts, inline amendment markers and Notas de Vigencia, as for the Constitution and the Código Civil. Where the source names an act but gives no text after it, the article reads '[La fuente no da el texto vigente en esta fecha.]' for that period.
- Constitutional Court rulings that struck text are laws of kind 'court', dated by the ruling's day as the source gives it; rulings that upheld text on a condition are court laws with a note and change no words.
- In-force days of amending acts: the act's own vigencia clause as its basedoc page gives it; otherwise the default rule (Ley 4 de 1913, art. 52: two months after promulgation). A day a provision's own note gives applies to that provision. The list below gives the day and the rule for each act.
- Dates come only from the act itself: the Diario Oficial day on the act's own page wins over a day the code's notes give; a note's Diario Oficial day is not given to an act named only inside another act's quoted title; and an act whose vigencia clause has a general rule and exceptions is dated by the rule that covers the changed provision (Ley 890 de 2004: arts. 7 to 13 immediately, the rest from 1 January 2005; Ley 2502 de 2025: art. 296 of the Código Penal one year after promulgation; Ley 2080 de 2021: from publication).
- The source's earlier texts often leave out the article's epigraph (its title); where the latest earlier text differs from today's only by that or by a change no note names, today's text is used from the latest named act's day. Articles where that happened: ley_0599_2000 art-0058, ley_0599_2000 art-0215, ley_0599_2000 art-0312, ley_0599_2000 art-0337, ley_0599_2000 art-0471, ley_1564_2012 art-0020, ley_1564_2012 art-0024, ley_1564_2012 art-0338, ley_1564_2012 art-0393, ley_1564_2012 art-0397, ley_1564_2012 art-0553, ley_1564_2012 art-0625, ley_1564_2012 art-0626, ley_1564_2012 art-0627.
- Código Penal: the figures of the penalties in the Parte Especial after 1 January 2005 are the publisher's: Ley 890 de 2004, art. 14, raised every penalty of the Parte Especial by a third in the minimum and a half in the maximum without rewriting the articles, and the source shows each article 'con las penas aumentadas' under the marker '<Penas aumentadas por el artículo 14 de la Ley 890 de 2004, a partir del 1o. de enero de 2005>', with the earlier wording in its Legislación Anterior. Those changes are credited to Ley 890 de 2004 on 1 January 2005; the source's own warning says the computation is the editor's reading of art. 14.
- Código General del Proceso: its first version is dated from promulgation (2012-07-12), the day the first of its articles took effect under art. 627 num. 1; the article itself (in the text) gives the later starts of the rest. Splitting the first version by article would need the Consejo Superior de la Judicatura's district-by-district acts, which the source does not give.

- Fallback audit (2026-10-31), summary: each of the 14 articles listed above as taking today's text from the latest named act's day was compared with the source's earlier text for that act; 10 of them had later words in an earlier version and now carry the source's earlier texts until the act or ruling that changed them (the decisions headed 'Fallback audit (2026-10-31)'); in the other 4 the difference is only the heading line, the source's punctuation or a slip in its transcription, or today's text does apply from that day, or the engine's later steps replaced that text, so no earlier version holds later words.
- Código Sustantivo del Trabajo: versions are rebuilt per article from the basedoc's earlier texts, inline amendment markers and Notas de Vigencia, as for the Constitution and the Código Civil. Where the source names an act but gives no text after it, the article reads '[La fuente no da el texto vigente en esta fecha.]' for that period.
- Constitutional Court and Corte Suprema rulings that struck text are laws of kind 'court', dated by the ruling's day as the source gives it; rulings that upheld text on a condition are court laws with a note and change no words.
- In-force days of amending acts: the act's own vigencia clause as its basedoc page gives it; otherwise the default rule (Ley 4 de 1913, art. 52: two months after promulgation). A day a provision's own note gives applies to that provision. The list below gives the day and the rule for each act.
- The source's earlier texts often leave out the article's epigraph (its title); where the latest earlier text differs from today's only by that or by a change no note names, today's text is used from the latest named act's day. Articles where that happened: codigo_sustantivo_trabajo art-0057, codigo_sustantivo_trabajo art-0059, codigo_sustantivo_trabajo art-0069, codigo_sustantivo_trabajo art-0099, codigo_sustantivo_trabajo art-0103, codigo_sustantivo_trabajo art-0119, codigo_sustantivo_trabajo art-0157, codigo_sustantivo_trabajo art-0200, codigo_sustantivo_trabajo art-0201, codigo_sustantivo_trabajo art-0231, codigo_sustantivo_trabajo art-0236, codigo_sustantivo_trabajo art-0266, codigo_sustantivo_trabajo art-0289, codigo_sustantivo_trabajo art-0347, codigo_sustantivo_trabajo art-0376, codigo_sustantivo_trabajo art-0453.
- Código Sustantivo del Trabajo: the oldest text the source gives is the official edition published in Diario Oficial No. 27.622 of 1951-06-07; it is the first version (credited as oldest version available, naming Decretos 2663 and 3743 de 1950 and 905 de 1951). Ley 50 de 1990, art. 107, replaced the word 'patrono' with 'empleador' throughout the code; the source shows the replaced word in braces ('{empleador}', its Resumen de Notas de Vigencia, note 74). Before 1991-01-01 those words read 'patrono(s)', from 1991-01-01 'empleador(es)', credited to Ley 50 de 1990; the braces are dropped.
- Código Sustantivo del Trabajo: Ley 171 de 1961 changed the pension rules in its own articles and, by its art. 14, repealed only arts. 261, 262 and 267 of the code. The source's notes 'Artículo modificado por la Ley 171 de 1961 ... según lo dispuesto en el artículo 14' on arts. 260, 266, 275 and 279 name no change of their words, and its 'Texto original de la Ley 171 de 1961' boxes on arts. 260 and 267 quote that act's own articles 6, 7 and 8; they are not read as texts of the code. Ley 171 de 1961 is in force from 1 February 1962 (its art. 15: 'desde el día 1o. del mes siguiente al de su promulgación'; Diario Oficial No. 30.696 of 16 January 1962 in the source's notes).
- Código Sustantivo del Trabajo: arts. 260, 273, 275, 277, 278 and 280 to 284 were repealed or replaced ('subrogado') by Ley 100 de 1993; the source still prints their original words under markers such as '<Artículo derogado por el artículo 289 de la Ley 100 de 1993. El texto derogado continua vigente para los trabajadores sometidos al régimen de transición creado por el artículo 36 de la Ley 100. El texto original es el es el siguiente:>' or '<Artículo subrogado por La ley 100 de 1993. Ver Notas del Editor. El texto original es el siguiente:>'. Those words are read as the articles' original text, and the articles are removed on Ley 100's in-force day, like arts. 268 to 272 that the same act repealed. Court rulings on the repealed words (art. 260: Sentencia C-862-06, on the text kept for the transition regime of Ley 100, art. 36) are not recorded.
- Código Sustantivo del Trabajo: articles the source shows with no words today but whose original words it gives in a 'Texto original' box keep those words until the act the source names for their end: arts. 210 and 211 until Decreto 1295 de 1994 (its art. 98 repealed them tacitly, as the Corte Constitucional's Sentencia C-184-10, quoted in the source's Notas del Editor, says); art. 346 until Ley 75 de 1986 ('derogado tácitamente por el artículo 35'); arts. 454 to 461 (labour arbitration) until 12 October 2012, when Ley 1563 de 2012, art. 118, repealed arts. 183 to 190 of Decreto 1818 de 1998, the compilation that had incorporated them; the source's marker gives that day. The source's own Notas del Editor add that the Corte Suprema de Justicia (AL2314-2014) holds the code's arbitration rules still in force; the history follows the source's current text, which has no words for them.
- Código Sustantivo del Trabajo: struck words or whole articles the source marks INEXEQUIBLE without naming a dated ruling (arts. 323 and 409, whole; the words of art. 247 that the Corte Constitucional, Sentencia C-823-06, found already repealed) are left out of every version. Art. 162, num. 1, lit. d) (repealed by Decreto 1393 de 1970, art. 56) reads as repealed in every version: the source gives that decree's Diario Oficial number (33.131) but no day, and no other source in reach gives its in-force day.
- Código Sustantivo del Trabajo: the Corte Suprema de Justicia's ruling of 18 July 1973 that struck the second sentence of art. 44 is a court law keyed csj-1973-07-18 (the source names it by its day only).
- Código Sustantivo del Trabajo: art. 200 (enfermedad profesional) was repealed by Decreto 1295 de 1994, art. 98, and revived by the Corte Constitucional's Sentencia C-1155-08 of 26 November 2008 ('la presente declaración de inexequibilidad revive el artículo 200 del Código Sustantivo del Trabajo', quoted in its Notas de Vigencia): absent from 24 June 1994, back with its original words from the ruling's day.
- Código Sustantivo del Trabajo, source slips fixed: headings whose anchor holds only 'A' or 'ART' (arts. 40, 266) or whose number stands in a marker (163A, 167A, 185-A, 235-A, 391-1, 481-1) are made whole; 'Decreo' and 'Decreto - Ley' read as 'Decreto' and 'Decreto Ley'; art. 357's 'Texto modificado por el Decreto 2351 de 1995' is Decreto 2351 de 1965 (its marker); '<Apartes tachados suprimidos por el artículo 53 de la Ley 962 de 2005>' (art. 73) is read as words that act removed; the publisher's cross-reference stubs ('l0146_94+[...]', art. 236) are dropped.

- Fallback audit (2026-10-31), summary: each of the 16 articles listed above as taking today's text from the latest named act's day was compared with the source's earlier text for that act; 4 of them had later words in an earlier version and now carry the source's earlier texts until the act or ruling that changed them (the decisions headed 'Fallback audit (2026-10-31)'); in the other 12 the difference is only the heading line, the source's punctuation or a slip in its transcription, or today's text does apply from that day, or the engine's later steps replaced that text, so no earlier version holds later words.
- Código de Comercio, Código de Procedimiento Administrativo, Código de Procedimiento Penal, Código de la Infancia: versions are rebuilt per article from the basedoc's earlier texts, inline amendment markers and Notas de Vigencia, as for the Constitution and the Código Civil. Where the source names an act but gives no text after it, the article reads '[La fuente no da el texto vigente en esta fecha.]' for that period.
- Constitutional Court rulings that struck text are laws of kind 'court', dated by the ruling's day as the source gives it; rulings that upheld text on a condition are court laws with a note and change no words.
- In-force days of amending acts: the act's own vigencia clause as its basedoc page gives it; otherwise the default rule (Ley 4 de 1913, art. 52: two months after promulgation). A day a provision's own note gives applies to that provision. The list below gives the day and the rule for each act.
- Dates come only from the act itself: the Diario Oficial day on the act's own page wins over a day the code's notes give; a note's Diario Oficial day is not given to an act named only inside another act's quoted title; and an act whose vigencia clause has a general rule and exceptions is dated by the rule that covers the changed provision (Ley 890 de 2004: arts. 7 to 13 immediately, the rest from 1 January 2005; Ley 2502 de 2025: art. 296 of the Código Penal one year after promulgation; Ley 2080 de 2021: from publication).
- The source's earlier texts often leave out the article's epigraph (its title); where the latest earlier text differs from today's only by that or by a change no note names, today's text is used from the latest named act's day. - Fallback audit (2026-10-31): Código de Procedimiento Penal (Ley 906 de 2004), Art. 317: from 2016-07-01: the source's earlier text (Ley 1786 de 2016); today's wording from 2016-08-19 (Fe de erratas, Diario Oficial No. 49.970 de 19 de agosto de 2016, numeral 5 as Ley 1786 de 2016 was first printed, corrected by the fe de erratas in Diario Oficial No. 49.970 of 19 August 2016).
- Fallback audit (2026-10-31): Código de Procedimiento Penal (Ley 906 de 2004), Art. 531: from 2004-09-01: the source's earlier text (Ley 906 de 2004); out of force from 2006-12-05 (Sentencia C-1033 de 2006, the article was declared INEXEQUIBLE by Sentencia C-1033-06 of 5 December 2006).
- Fallback audit (2026-10-31): Código de la Infancia y la Adolescencia (Ley 1098 de 2006), Art. 32: from 2006-11-15: the source's earlier text (Decreto 4011 de 2006); today's wording from 2007-03-02 (Decreto 578 de 2007, inciso 3 as corrected by Decreto 4011 de 2006, which Decreto 578 de 2007 repealed; today the page prints the act's own wording).
- Fallback audit (2026-10-31): Código de Procedimiento Administrativo y de lo Contencioso Administrativo (Ley 1437 de 2011), Art. 109: from 2012-07-02: the source's earlier text (Ley 1437 de 2011); today's wording from 2015-07-01 (Acto Legislativo 2 de 2015, numeral 5 is marked 'derogado tácitamente por el artículo 22 del Acto Legislativo 2 de 2015' (in force from 2015-07-01)).
- Fallback audit (2026-10-31), summary: each of the 27 articles listed above as taking today's text from the latest named act's day was compared with the source's earlier text for that act; 6 of them had later words in an earlier version and now carry the source's earlier texts until the act or ruling that changed them (the decisions headed 'Fallback audit (2026-10-31)'); in the other 21 the difference is only the heading line, the source's punctuation or a slip in its transcription, or today's text does apply from that day, or the engine's later steps replaced that text, so no earlier version holds later words.
- Código de Comercio (Decreto 410 de 1971): article words the page puts under anchors named like headings ('Nivel001', 'TITULO I', ...) had been read as headings, so they were missing from their articles (in titulos.md, or nowhere); they are back in them: art. 441 (its heading in the text, under an anchor named 'Nivel001').
- Código Nacional de Seguridad y Convivencia Ciudadana, Ley 100 de 1993, Código General Disciplinario, Código Electoral: versions are rebuilt per article from the basedoc's earlier texts, inline amendment markers and Notas de Vigencia, as for the Constitution and the Código Civil. Where the source names an act but gives no text after it, the article reads '[La fuente no da el texto vigente en esta fecha.]' for that period.
- Constitutional Court rulings that struck text are laws of kind 'court', dated by the ruling's day as the source gives it; rulings that upheld text on a condition are court laws with a note and change no words.
- In-force days of amending acts: the act's own vigencia clause as its basedoc page gives it; otherwise the default rule (Ley 4 de 1913, art. 52: two months after promulgation). A day a provision's own note gives applies to that provision. The list below gives the day and the rule for each act.
- Dates come only from the act itself: the Diario Oficial day on the act's own page wins over a day the code's notes give; a note's Diario Oficial day is not given to an act named only inside another act's quoted title; and an act whose vigencia clause has a general rule and exceptions is dated by the rule that covers the changed provision (Ley 890 de 2004: arts. 7 to 13 immediately, the rest from 1 January 2005; Ley 2502 de 2025: art. 296 of the Código Penal one year after promulgation; Ley 2080 de 2021: from publication).
- The source's earlier texts often leave out the article's epigraph (its title); where the latest earlier text differs from today's only by that or by a change no note names, today's text is used from the latest named act's day. Articles where that happened: decreto_2241_1986 art-0092, ley_0100_1993 art-0026, ley_0100_1993 art-0041, ley_0100_1993 art-0054, ley_0100_1993 art-0094, ley_0100_1993 art-0115, ley_0100_1993 art-0135, ley_0100_1993 art-0221, ley_0100_1993 art-0264, ley_0100_1993 art-0265, ley_0100_1993 art-0266, ley_1801_2016 art-0093, ley_1801_2016 art-0126, ley_1801_2016 art-0127, ley_1801_2016 art-0128, ley_1801_2016 art-0162, ley_1952_2019 art-0100, ley_1952_2019 art-0225d, ley_1952_2019 art-0236, ley_1952_2019 art-0238a, ley_1952_2019 art-0239, ley_1952_2019 art-0263.
- Código Nacional de Seguridad y Convivencia Ciudadana: Ley 1801 de 2016, art. 41: its earlier text is headed 'Texto original de la Ley 1810 de 2016'; it is the code's own original paragraph 3 (Ley 1810 de 2016 is an unrelated act), struck by Sentencia C-281 de 2017
- Código Nacional de Seguridad y Convivencia Ciudadana: Ley 1801 de 2016, art. 88: its earlier text is headed 'Texto original de la Ley 1805 de 2016'; it is the code's own original article (Ley 1805 de 2016 is an unrelated act)
- Código Nacional de Seguridad y Convivencia Ciudadana: Ley 1801 de 2016, art. 206: its earlier text is headed 'Texto original de la Ley 906 de 2004'; it is the code's own original article (the Código de Procedimiento Penal never held it; the same article's notes name only Leyes 2030 de 2020 and 2492 de 2025 as its amenders)
- Ley 100 de 1993: Ley 100 de 1993, arts. 204 and 214: their earlier texts are headed 'Texto original de la Ley 100 de 2007'; they are the act's own original wording (there is no Ley 100 de 2007 in these notes; the next text is Ley 1122 de 2007's)
- Ley 100 de 1993: Ley 100 de 1993, art. 157: the earlier text of its paragraph 4 is headed 'Texto original del Decreto 2277 de 1979' (the teachers' statute, which never held it); it is the act's own original paragraph
- Ley 100 de 1993: Ley 100 de 1993, arts. 215 and 216: the note 'Artículo modificado parcialmente por el Decreto 132 de 2010, según lo dispuesto en su artículo 5' names an emergency decree (struck by Sentencia C-374 de 2010) that changed none of their words and that the source gives no text for; their wording is unbroken
- Ley 100 de 1993: Ley 100 de 1993, art. 271: Decreto 1280 de 2002, art. 9, gave the competence the article assigns to the Ministerio de Salud to the Superintendencia Nacional de Salud without rewriting it (the note quotes it); the article's wording is unbroken
- Código Nacional de Seguridad y Convivencia Ciudadana: Sentencia C-223 de 2017 (20 April 2017) declared Título VI of the Código Nacional de Seguridad y Convivencia Ciudadana (arts. 47 to 75, the right of assembly) INEXEQUIBLE, deferring the effect 'hasta antes del 20 de junio de 2019' so that Congress could pass a statutory law; the articles leave the text on 20 June 2019. The commit is dated from the end of the deferral; the law's enacted day is the decision's.
- Código General Disciplinario: Decreto 1656 de 2021 corrected errors in Ley 2094 de 2021 (Diario Oficial No. 51.880 of 6 December 2021); the basedoc does not hold it. Its corrections here (arts. 225D and 238A of the Código General Disciplinario) are to articles Ley 2094 de 2021 added, which took effect on 29 March 2022 (its art. 73), so they are shown from that day (no text before the act that inserted it).
- Código General Disciplinario: Código General Disciplinario, art. 52: the word 'grave' (num. 1 lit. b) was struck by Sentencia C-560 de 2019 (20 November 2019), which applied Sentencia C-181 de 2002 on the same words of Ley 200 de 1995; the 2002 ruling and C-1076 de 2002 (on Ley 734 de 2002) are about earlier acts and are not credited
- Código Nacional de Seguridad y Convivencia Ciudadana (Ley 1801 de 2016): first version from 2017-01-29: Ley 1801 de 2016, art. 243: 'La presente ley regirá seis (6) meses después de su promulgación.' (published 2016-07-29)
- Ley 100 de 1993 (Sistema de Seguridad Social Integral): first version from 1993-12-23: Ley 100 de 1993, art. 289: 'La presente Ley rige a partir de la fecha de su publicación, salvaguarda los derechos adquiridos y deroga todas las disposiciones que le sean contrarias, en especial el artículo 2o. de la Ley 4a. de 1966, el artículo 5o. de la Ley 33 de 1985, el parágrafo del art. 7o. de la Ley 71 de 1988, los artículos 260, 268, 269, 270, 271 y 272 del Código de Sustantivo del Trabajo y demás normas que los modifiquen o adicionen.'
- Código General Disciplinario (Ley 1952 de 2019): first version from 2019-01-28: Ley 1952 de 2019, art. 265 as enacted: 'La presente ley entrará a regir cuatro (4) meses después de su sanción y publicación ...' (arts. 33, 101, 102, 208 to 235 and 254 eighteen months after promulgation); postponed to 1 July 2021 by Ley 1955 de 2019, art. 140, and replaced by Ley 2094 de 2021, art. 73: 'Las disposiciones previstas en la presente ley, y las contenidas en la Ley 1952 de 2019, que no son objeto de reforma, entrarán a regir nueve (9) meses después de su promulgación. Durante este período conservará su vigencia plena la Ley 734 de 2002, con sus reformas. Deróguese el artículo 248 de la Ley 1952 de 2019 y deróguese la referencia a las palabras "y la consulta" que está prevista en el numeral 1 del artículo 59 de la ley 1123 de 2007. Los regímenes especiales en materia disciplinaria y las norma relacionadas con la Comisión de Ética del Congreso conservarán su vigencia. PARÁGRAFO 1o. '. The first version is dated from promulgation (2019-01-28).

La presente rige a partir de su promulgación y publicación en el Diario Oficial y deroga todas las disposiciones que le sean contrarias, en especial el Título IX "Disposiciones generales" y el artículo 58 de la Ley 84 de 1989.').
- In force 2025-07-23: Ley 2492 de 2025 (Ley 2492 de 2025: 'ARTÍCULO 11. VIGENCIA. La presente ley rige a partir de su promulgación y deroga todas aquellas disposiciones que le sean contrarias.').
- In force 2026-09-15: Ley 2633 de 2026 (Ley 2633 de 2026: 'ARTÍCULO 3o. VIGENCIA Y DEROGATORIAS. La presente ley rige a partir de su sanción, promulgación y publicación en el Diario Oficial y deroga las disposiciones que le sean contrarias.').
- Ley 962 de 2005 is dated 8 July 2005, its first publication, as the other parts of this corpus date it: Ley 962 de 2005, art. 86: 'La presente ley rige a partir de su publicación' (its page: 'Versión original publicada en el Diario Oficial No. 45.963 de 08 de julio de 2005'; the page prints the version republished in Diario Oficial No. 46.023 of 6 September 2005 with the corrections of Decreto 3075 de 2005).
- Ley 860 de 2003 is dated 2003-12-29 from its own words, which the general reading missed: Ley 860 de 2003, art. 5o: 'La presente ley empieza a regir a partir de su promulgación' (Diario Oficial of 2003-12-29).
- Sistema de Seguridad Social Integral (Ley 100 de 1993): words the source shows replaced in place (art. 41: '<S>y minusvalía</S> <e invalidez*>', with the marker 'Texto corregido en los términos de la Sentencia C-458-15') read as the old words before 2015-07-22 and as the new words from it, credited to Sentencia C-458 de 2015.
- Headings (titulos.md): the publisher's page links 'Anterior | Siguiente' that the basedoc prints after the last heading of a page are not words of that heading and are left out.
- Signature blocks at the end of the last article ('El Presidente del Honorable Senado de la República, ...', 'La Presidenta del honorable Senado ...', 'Publíquese y cúmplase. Dado en ...') are not part of the text and are cut: codigo-general-disciplinario art-0265; sistema-de-seguridad-social-integral art-0289.

- Fallback audit (2026-10-31), summary: each of the 22 articles listed above as taking today's text from the latest named act's day was compared with the source's earlier text for that act; 11 of them had later words in an earlier version and now carry the source's earlier texts until the act or ruling that changed them (the decisions headed 'Fallback audit (2026-10-31)'); in the other 11 the difference is only the heading line, the source's punctuation or a slip in its transcription, or today's text does apply from that day, or the engine's later steps replaced that text, so no earlier version holds later words.
- Código Nacional de Seguridad y Convivencia Ciudadana: article words the page puts under anchors named like headings ('Nivel001', 'TITULO I', ...) had been read as headings, so they were missing from their articles (in titulos.md, or nowhere); they are back in them: art. 5 (in the heading unit 'CAPÍTULO II-I-I' after the heading's words).
- Estatuto Tributario, Estatuto General de Contratación, Estatuto del Consumidor, Código Nacional de Tránsito: versions are rebuilt per article from the basedoc's earlier texts, inline amendment markers and Notas de Vigencia, as for the Constitution and the Código Civil. Where the source names an act but gives no text after it, the article reads '[La fuente no da el texto vigente en esta fecha.]' for that period.
- Constitutional Court rulings that struck text are laws of kind 'court', dated by the ruling's day as the source gives it; rulings that upheld text on a condition are court laws with a note and change no words.
- In-force days of amending acts: the act's own vigencia clause as its basedoc page gives it; otherwise the default rule (Ley 4 de 1913, art. 52: two months after promulgation). A day a provision's own note gives applies to that provision. The list below gives the day and the rule for each act.
- Dates come only from the act itself: the Diario Oficial day on the act's own page wins over a day the code's notes give; a note's Diario Oficial day is not given to an act named only inside another act's quoted title; and an act whose vigencia clause has a general rule and exceptions is dated by the rule that covers the changed provision (Ley 890 de 2004: arts. 7 to 13 immediately, the rest from 1 January 2005; Ley 2502 de 2025: art. 296 of the Código Penal one year after promulgation; Ley 2080 de 2021: from publication).
- The source's earlier texts often leave out the article's epigraph (its title); where the latest earlier text differs from today's only by that or by a change no note names, today's text is used from the latest named act's day. A partir de la promulgación de la presente ley, entrarán a regir el parágrafo del artículo 2o.; el literal l) del numeral 1o. y el numeral 9o. del artículo 24; las normas de este estatuto relacionadas con el contrato de concesión; el numeral 8o. del artículo 25; el numeral 5o., del artículo 32 sobre fiducia pública y encargo fiduciario; y los artículos 33, 34, 35' The first version is dated from promulgation (1993-10-28), when the parts the article names took effect; the rest from 1 January 1994.

- Ley 1239 de 2008 is dated 2008-07-25 from its own words, which the general reading missed: Ley 1239 de 2008, art. 4o: 'La presente ley tiene vigencia a partir de la fecha de su promulgación' (Diario Oficial of 2008-07-25).
- Headings (titulos.md): the publisher's page links 'Anterior | Siguiente' that the basedoc prints after the last heading of a page are not words of that heading and are left out.

- Ley 716 de 2001 is dated 2001-12-29 from its art. 21 ('La presente ley rige a partir de la fecha de su publicación', Diario Oficial No. 44.661 of 29 December 2001); the four Estatuto Tributario articles it changed (arts. 147, 505, 555-1, 618-1; its arts. 20, 14, 16, 15) hold its words from that day until Sentencia C-886 de 2002 (22 October 2002) struck them; art. 555-1 then reads its 1990 text until Ley 788 de 2002, and Ley 863 de 2003 repealed its inciso 4.
- Estatuto Tributario, arts. 903 to 916 (Libro Octavo 'Monotributo', added by Ley 1819 de 2016, art. 165): the Estatuto's page refers to Ley 1819 de 2016, art. 165 for their text ('Consultar texto original del Libro Octavo Monotributo directamente en el artículo 165 Ley 1819 de 2016'); that article's text on the act's own basedoc page stands from 2016-12-29 until Ley 1943 de 2018 replaced the Libro on 2018-12-28 (art. 908's table of categories left out, as tables are everywhere).
- Fallback audit (2026-10-31), summary: each of the 130 articles listed above as taking today's text from the latest named act's day was compared with the source's earlier text for that act; 54 of them had later words in an earlier version and now carry the source's earlier texts until the act or ruling that changed them (the decisions headed 'Fallback audit (2026-10-31)'); in the other 76 the difference is only the heading line, the source's punctuation or a slip in its transcription, or today's text does apply from that day, or the engine's later steps replaced that text, so no earlier version holds later words; except, left as they are because the source does not settle them: Art. 253 ('Inciso derogado tácitamente': no act or ruling dates it; only the editor's note cites a DIAN opinion (Ley 812 de 2003 art. 31, repealed by Ley 1151 de 2007 art. 160)).
- Estatuto Orgánico del Sistema Financiero, Código de Minas, Código Procesal del Trabajo, Régimen de Insolvencia Empresarial, Régimen de los Servicios Públicos Domiciliarios: versions are rebuilt per article from the basedoc's earlier texts, inline amendment markers and Notas de Vigencia, as for the Constitution and the Código Civil. Where the source names an act but gives no text after it, the article reads '[La fuente no da el texto vigente en esta fecha.]' for that period.
- Constitutional Court rulings that struck text are laws of kind 'court', dated by the ruling's day as the source gives it; rulings that upheld text on a condition are court laws with a note and change no words.
- In-force days of amending acts: the act's own vigencia clause as its basedoc page gives it; otherwise the default rule (Ley 4 de 1913, art. 52: two months after promulgation). A day a provision's own note gives applies to that provision. The list below gives the day and the rule for each act.
- Dates come only from the act itself: the Diario Oficial day on the act's own page wins over a day the code's notes give; a note's Diario Oficial day is not given to an act named only inside another act's quoted title; and an act whose vigencia clause has a general rule and exceptions is dated by the rule that covers the changed provision (Ley 890 de 2004: arts. 7 to 13 immediately, the rest from 1 January 2005; Ley 2502 de 2025: art. 296 of the Código Penal one year after promulgation; Ley 2080 de 2021: from publication).
- The source's earlier texts often leave out the article's epigraph (its title); where the latest earlier text differs from today's only by that or by a change no note names, today's text is used from the latest named act's day. Articles where that happened: codigo_procedimental_laboral art-0041, codigo_procedimental_laboral art-0086, codigo_procedimental_laboral art-0087, codigo_procedimental_laboral art-0131, estatuto_organico_sistema_financiero art-0074, estatuto_organico_sistema_financiero art-0151, estatuto_organico_sistema_financiero art-0211, estatuto_organico_sistema_financiero art-0220, estatuto_organico_sistema_financiero art-0228, estatuto_organico_sistema_financiero art-0230, estatuto_organico_sistema_financiero art-0231, estatuto_organico_sistema_financiero art-0326, estatuto_organico_sistema_financiero art-0327, estatuto_organico_sistema_financiero art-0328, estatuto_organico_sistema_financiero art-0329, estatuto_organico_sistema_financiero art-0330, estatuto_organico_sistema_financiero art-0332, estatuto_organico_sistema_financiero art-0333, estatuto_organico_sistema_financiero art-0334, ley_0142_1994 art-0001, ley_0142_1994 art-0009, ley_0142_1994 art-0014, ley_0142_1994 art-0016, ley_0142_1994 art-0024, ley_0142_1994 art-0025, ley_0142_1994 art-0032, ley_0142_1994 art-0058, ley_0142_1994 art-0099, ley_0142_1994 art-0125, ley_1116_2006 art-0010.

A partir de la promulgación de la presente ley, se prorroga la Ley 550 de 1999 por seis (6) meses y vencido dicho término, se aplicará de forma permanente solo a las entidades de que trata el artículo anterior de esta ley. Las normas del régimen establecido en la presente ley prevalecerán sobre cualquier otra de carácter ordinario que le sea contraria.' - Signature blocks at the end of the last article ('El Presidente del Honorable Senado de la República, ...', 'La Presidenta del honorable Senado ...', 'Publíquese y cúmplase. - Fallback audit (2026-10-31), summary: each of the 30 articles listed above as taking today's text from the latest named act's day was compared with the source's earlier text for that act; 17 of them had later words in an earlier version and now carry the source's earlier texts until the act or ruling that changed them (the decisions headed 'Fallback audit (2026-10-31)'); in the other 13 the difference is only the heading line, the source's punctuation or a slip in its transcription, or today's text does apply from that day, or the engine's later steps replaced that text, so no earlier version holds later words.
- Estatuto Orgánico del Sistema Financiero: article words the page puts under anchors named like headings ('Nivel001', 'TITULO I', ...) had been read as headings, so they were missing from their articles (in titulos.md, or nowhere); they are back in them: art. 112 (2 blocks under anchors named 'Nivel001'); art. 183 (1 block under an anchor named 'Nivel001'); art. 193 (2 blocks under anchors named 'Nivel001'); art. 218 (9 blocks under anchors named 'Nivel001'); art. 270 (2 blocks under anchors named 'Nivel001').
- Ley 1150 de 2007, eficiencia y transparencia en la contratación pública, Estatuto Anticorrupción (Ley 1474 de 2011), Ley de Transparencia y del Derecho de Acceso a la Información Pública Nacional (Ley 1712 de 2014), Ley 789 de 2002, sistema de protección social y reforma laboral, Ley de Formalización y Generación de Empleo (Ley 1429 de 2010), Política Nacional de Gestión del Riesgo de Desastres (Ley 1523 de 2012), Ley General Ambiental (Ley 99 de 1993), Ley de Desarrollo Territorial (Ley 388 de 1997), Código Nacional de Recursos Naturales Renovables y de Protección al Medio Ambiente (Decreto Ley 2811 de 1974): versions are rebuilt per article from the basedoc's earlier texts, inline amendment markers and Notas de Vigencia, as for the Constitution and the Código Civil. Where the source names an act but gives no text after it, the article reads '[La fuente no da el texto vigente en esta fecha.]' for that period.
- Constitutional Court rulings that struck text are laws of kind 'court', dated by the ruling's day as the source gives it; rulings that upheld text on a condition are court laws with a note and change no words.
- In-force days of amending acts: the act's own vigencia clause as its basedoc page gives it; otherwise the default rule (Ley 4 de 1913, art. 52: two months after promulgation). A day a provision's own note gives applies to that provision. The list below gives the day and the rule for each act.
- Dates come only from the act itself: the Diario Oficial day on the act's own page wins over a day the code's notes give; a note's Diario Oficial day is not given to an act named only inside another act's quoted title; and an act whose vigencia clause has a general rule and exceptions is dated by the rule that covers the changed provision (Ley 890 de 2004: arts. 7 to 13 immediately, the rest from 1 January 2005; Ley 2502 de 2025: art. 296 of the Código Penal one year after promulgation; Ley 2080 de 2021: from publication).
- The source's earlier texts often leave out the article's epigraph (its title); where the latest earlier text differs from today's only by that or by a change no note names, today's text is used from the latest named act's day. Articles where that happened: ley_0099_1993 art-0011, ley_0099_1993 art-0033, ley_0099_1993 art-0036, ley_0099_1993 art-0049, ley_0099_1993 art-0052, ley_0099_1993 art-0056, ley_0388_1997 art-0071, ley_0789_2002 art-0019, ley_0789_2002 art-0020, ley_0789_2002 art-0044, ley_1150_2007 art-0002, ley_1150_2007 art-0030.

- Ley 1150 de 2007, eficiencia y transparencia en la contratación pública: first version from 2007-07-16: Ley 1150 de 2007, art. 33: 'La presente ley empieza a regir seis (6) meses después de su promulgación, con excepción del artículo 6o que entrará a regir a los dieciocho (18) meses de su promulgación. PARÁGRAFO 1o. En tanto no entre en vigor el artículo 6o de la presente ley las entidades podrán verificar la información de los proponentes a que se refiere el numeral 1 del artículo 5o de la presente ley. PARÁGRAFO 2o. Los artículos 9o y 17 entrarán a regir una vez se promulgue la presente ley.' The first version is dated from promulgation (2007-07-16), when arts. 9 and 17 took effect; the rest six months later (2008-01-16), art. 6 eighteen months later (2009-01-16).
- Ley 1150 de 2007, eficiencia y transparencia en la contratación pública: art. 3, parágrafo 2: its marker reads 'Parágrafo derogado por el artículo 223 del Decreto 19 de 2012, a partir del 1o. de junio de 2012'; the repeal is dated 1 June 2012, not from the decree's publication.
- Estatuto Anticorrupción (Ley 1474 de 2011): first version from 2011-07-12: Ley 1474 de 2011, art. 135: 'La presente ley rige a partir de su promulgación y deroga las normas que le sean contrarias.' (sanctioned 2011-07-12, Diario Oficial of 2011-07-12)
- Ley de Transparencia y del Derecho de Acceso a la Información Pública Nacional (Ley 1712 de 2014): first version from 2014-09-06: Ley 1712 de 2014, art. 33: 'La presente ley rige a los seis (6) meses de la fecha de su promulgación para todos los sujetos obligados del orden nacional. Para los entes territoriales la ley entrará en vigencia un año después de su promulgación. La presente ley deroga todas las disposiciones que le sean contrarias.' The first version is dated six months after promulgation (2014-09-06), when it took effect for national bodies; for territorial bodies a year after (2015-03-06).
- Ley 789 de 2002, sistema de protección social y reforma laboral: first version from 2002-12-27: Ley 789 de 2002, art. 52: 'La presente ley rige a partir de la fecha de su publicación y deroga las disposiciones que le sean contrarias. Derógase el artículo 77 de la Ley 101 de 1993. Deróguense en particular los incisos 3o. y 4o. del parágrafo único del artículo 181 de la Ley 223 de 1995 en lo tocante a mantener la exoneración del pago al SENA excepto para las Universidades Públicas.' (sanctioned 2002-12-27, Diario Oficial of 2002-12-27)
- Ley 789 de 2002, sistema de protección social y reforma laboral: art. 30 is printed under '<Artículo derogado y tema INCORPORADO como artículo 81 al Código Sustantivo del Trabajo por el artículo 21 de la Ley 2466 de 2025>'; its text is kept until Ley 2466 de 2025 takes effect (2025-06-25) and the article is removed that day.
- Ley de Formalización y Generación de Empleo (Ley 1429 de 2010): first version from 2010-12-29: Ley 1429 de 2010, art. 65: 'La presente ley rige a partir de la fecha de su publicación y promulgación y deroga o modifica las disposiciones que le sean contrarias. PARÁGRAFO 1o. Los beneficios de progresividad de que tratan el artículo 5o y 7o de la presente ley tendrán vigencia hasta el 31 de diciembre del año dos mil catorce (2014). PARÁGRAFO 2o. Registro Comité Paritario de Salud Ocupacional. Suprímase el literal f) del artículo 21 del Decreto-ley 1295 de 1994. PARÁGRAFO 3o. Derogatorias del Código Sustantivo del Trabajo. Deróguese las siguientes disposiciones y artículos del Código Sustantivo del Trabajo: 72, 74, 75' (sanctioned 2010-12-29, Diario Oficial of 2010-12-29)
- Política Nacional de Gestión del Riesgo de Desastres (Ley 1523 de 2012): first version from 2012-04-24: Ley 1523 de 2012, art. 96: 'La presente ley rige a partir de la fecha de su publicación y deroga las disposiciones que le sean contrarias, en especial la Ley 46 de 1988 y el Decreto-ley 919 de 1989, con excepción de lo dispuesto en el inciso primero del artículo 70 del Decreto-ley, así como también los artículos 1o inciso primero, 2o y 3o del Decreto 1547 de 1984, modificado por el Decreto-ley 919 de 1989. De igual manera, deróguense los artículos 1o y 5o a excepción del parágrafo 2o; los artículos 6o, 7o y 8o del Decreto 4702 de 2010; el artículo 2o del Decreto 4830 de 2010 y mantendrán plena vigencia los artículos 2o, ' (sanctioned 2012-04-24, Diario Oficial of 2012-04-24)

- Fallback audit (2026-10-31), summary: each of the 12 articles listed above as taking today's text from the latest named act's day was compared with the source's earlier text for that act; 10 of them had later words in an earlier version and now carry the source's earlier texts until the act or ruling that changed them (the decisions headed 'Fallback audit (2026-10-31)'); in the other 2 the difference is only the heading line, the source's punctuation or a slip in its transcription, or today's text does apply from that day, or the engine's later steps replaced that text, so no earlier version holds later words.
- Ley Estatutaria de la Administración de Justicia (Ley 270 de 1996), Código Disciplinario Único (Ley 734 de 2002), Código Penitenciario y Carcelario (Ley 65 de 1993), Ley de Víctimas y Restitución de Tierras (Ley 1448 de 2011), Ley General de Educación (Ley 115 de 1994), Servicio Público de la Educación Superior (Ley 30 de 1992), Régimen Municipal (Ley 136 de 1994), Sociedad por Acciones Simplificada (Ley 1258 de 2008), Ley de Hábeas Data (Ley 1266 de 2008), Ley de Comercio Electrónico (Ley 527 de 1999): versions are rebuilt per article from the basedoc's earlier texts, inline amendment markers and Notas de Vigencia, as for the Constitution and the Código Civil. Where the source names an act but gives no text after it, the article reads '[La fuente no da el texto vigente en esta fecha.]' for that period.
- Constitutional Court rulings that struck text are laws of kind 'court', dated by the ruling's day as the source gives it; rulings that upheld text on a condition are court laws with a note and change no words.
- In-force days of amending acts: the act's own vigencia clause as its basedoc page gives it; otherwise the default rule (Ley 4 de 1913, art. 52: two months after promulgation). A day a provision's own note gives applies to that provision. The list below gives the day and the rule for each act.
- Dates come only from the act itself: the Diario Oficial day on the act's own page wins over a day the code's notes give; a note's Diario Oficial day is not given to an act named only inside another act's quoted title; and an act whose vigencia clause has a general rule and exceptions is dated by the rule that covers the changed provision (Ley 890 de 2004: arts. 7 to 13 immediately, the rest from 1 January 2005; Ley 2502 de 2025: art. 296 of the Código Penal one year after promulgation; Ley 2080 de 2021: from publication).
- The source's earlier texts often leave out the article's epigraph (its title); where the latest earlier text differs from today's only by that or by a change no note names, today's text is used from the latest named act's day. - Sociedad por Acciones Simplificada (Ley 1258 de 2008): first version from 2008-12-05: Ley 1258 de 2008, art. 46: 'La presente ley rige a partir del momento de su promulgación y deroga todas las disposiciones que le sean contrarias. - Fallback audit (2026-10-31), summary: each of the 49 articles listed above as taking today's text from the latest named act's day was compared with the source's earlier text for that act; 46 of them had later words in an earlier version and now carry the source's earlier texts until the act or ruling that changed them (the decisions headed 'Fallback audit (2026-10-31)'); in the other 3 the difference is only the heading line, the source's punctuation or a slip in its transcription, or today's text does apply from that day, or the engine's later steps replaced that text, so no earlier version holds later words.
- Ley General de Educación (Ley 115 de 1994), arts. 1 and 46: the words Sentencia C-458 de 2015 ordered read ('personas en situación de discapacidad física, sensorial y psíquica'), which the page gives in angle brackets under the marker '<Texto(s) entre <> corregido(s) en los términos de la Sentencia C-458-15>', are the text from 22 July 2015; the marker is the publisher's.
- Ley Estatutaria de la Administración de Justicia (Ley 270 de 1996): the article without a number that Ley 1285 de 2009, art. 25, added after art. 209B (the page heads it 'ARTÍCULO NUEVO.') is the file art-0209-nuevo-1, in force from 2009-01-22.
- Reglamento del Congreso (Ley 5 de 1992): versions are rebuilt per article from the basedoc's earlier texts, inline amendment markers and Notas de Vigencia, as for the Constitution and the Código Civil. Where the source names an act but gives no text after it, the article reads '[La fuente no da el texto vigente en esta fecha.]' for that period.
- Constitutional Court rulings that struck text are laws of kind 'court', dated by the ruling's day as the source gives it; rulings that upheld text on a condition are court laws with a note and change no words.
- In-force days of amending acts: the act's own vigencia clause as its basedoc page gives it; otherwise the default rule (Ley 4 de 1913, art. 52: two months after promulgation). A day a provision's own note gives applies to that provision. The list below gives the day and the rule for each act.
- Dates come only from the act itself: the Diario Oficial day on the act's own page wins over a day the code's notes give; a note's Diario Oficial day is not given to an act named only inside another act's quoted title; and an act whose vigencia clause has a general rule and exceptions is dated by the rule that covers the changed provision (Ley 890 de 2004: arts. 7 to 13 immediately, the rest from 1 January 2005; Ley 2502 de 2025: art. 296 of the Código Penal one year after promulgation; Ley 2080 de 2021: from publication).
- The source's earlier texts often leave out the article's epigraph (its title); where the latest earlier text differs from today's only by that or by a change no note names, today's text is used from the latest named act's day. Articles where that happened: ley_0005_1992 art-0273, ley_0005_1992 art-0329, ley_0005_1992 art-0330, ley_0005_1992 art-0331, ley_0005_1992 art-0332, ley_0005_1992 art-0347, ley_0005_1992 art-0388.

- Fallback audit (2026-10-31), summary: each of the 7 articles listed above as taking today's text from the latest named act's day was compared with the source's earlier text for that act; 7 of them had later words in an earlier version and now carry the source's earlier texts until the act or ruling that changed them (the decisions headed 'Fallback audit (2026-10-31)'); in the other 0 the difference is only the heading line, the source's punctuation or a slip in its transcription, or today's text does apply from that day, or the engine's later steps replaced that text, so no earlier version holds later words.
- Reglamento del Congreso (Ley 5 de 1992): article words the page puts under anchors named like headings ('Nivel001', 'TITULO I', ...) had been read as headings, so they were missing from their articles (in titulos.md, or nowhere); they are back in them: art. 309 (its heading spelt 'ARTICUL0', under an anchor named 'Nivel-309').
- Acción de tutela (Decreto 2591 de 1991), Acciones populares y de grupo (Ley 472 de 1998), Acción de cumplimiento (Ley 393 de 1997), Código Disciplinario del Abogado (Ley 1123 de 2007), Código de Extinción de Dominio (Ley 1708 de 2014), Estatuto de Arbitraje Nacional e Internacional (Ley 1563 de 2012), Estatuto de Conciliación (Ley 2220 de 2022), Código de Procedimiento Penal de 2000 (Ley 600 de 2000), Responsabilidad fiscal (Ley 610 de 2000), Mecanismos de participación ciudadana (Ley 134 de 1994): versions are rebuilt per article from the basedoc's earlier texts, inline amendment markers and Notas de Vigencia, as for the Constitution and the Código Civil. Where the source names an act but gives no text after it, the article reads '[La fuente no da el texto vigente en esta fecha.]' for that period.
- Constitutional Court rulings that struck text are laws of kind 'court', dated by the ruling's day as the source gives it; rulings that upheld text on a condition are court laws with a note and change no words.
- In-force days of amending acts: the act's own vigencia clause as its basedoc page gives it; otherwise the default rule (Ley 4 de 1913, art. 52: two months after promulgation). A day a provision's own note gives applies to that provision. The list below gives the day and the rule for each act.
- Dates come only from the act itself: the Diario Oficial day on the act's own page wins over a day the code's notes give; a note's Diario Oficial day is not given to an act named only inside another act's quoted title; and an act whose vigencia clause has a general rule and exceptions is dated by the rule that covers the changed provision (Ley 890 de 2004: arts. 7 to 13 immediately, the rest from 1 January 2005; Ley 2502 de 2025: art. 296 of the Código Penal one year after promulgation; Ley 2080 de 2021: from publication).
- The source's earlier texts often leave out the article's epigraph (its title); where the latest earlier text differs from today's only by that or by a change no note names, today's text is used from the latest named act's day. - Fallback audit (2026-10-31), summary: each of the 28 articles listed above as taking today's text from the latest named act's day was compared with the source's earlier text for that act; 11 of them had later words in an earlier version and now carry the source's earlier texts until the act or ruling that changed them (the decisions headed 'Fallback audit (2026-10-31)'); in the other 17 the difference is only the heading line, the source's punctuation or a slip in its transcription, or today's text does apply from that day, or the engine's later steps replaced that text, so no earlier version holds later words.
- Organización y funcionamiento de la administración pública (Ley 489 de 1998), Empleo público y carrera administrativa (Ley 909 de 2004), Ley Orgánica del Plan de Desarrollo (Ley 152 de 1994), Ley Orgánica de Ordenamiento Territorial (Ley 1454 de 2011), Ley 617 de 2000 (categorías y gasto de las entidades territoriales), Sistema General de Participaciones (Ley 715 de 2001), Control fiscal (Ley 42 de 1993), Participación democrática (Ley 1757 de 2015), Estatuto Orgánico del Presupuesto (Decreto 111 de 1996), Estatuto Orgánico de Bogotá (Decreto 1421 de 1993): versions are rebuilt per article from the basedoc's earlier texts, inline amendment markers and Notas de Vigencia, as for the Constitution and the Código Civil. Where the source names an act but gives no text after it, the article reads '[La fuente no da el texto vigente en esta fecha.]' for that period.
- Constitutional Court rulings that struck text are laws of kind 'court', dated by the ruling's day as the source gives it; rulings that upheld text on a condition are court laws with a note and change no words.
- In-force days of amending acts: the act's own vigencia clause as its basedoc page gives it; otherwise the default rule (Ley 4 de 1913, art. 52: two months after promulgation). A day a provision's own note gives applies to that provision. The list below gives the day and the rule for each act.
- Dates come only from the act itself: the Diario Oficial day on the act's own page wins over a day the code's notes give; a note's Diario Oficial day is not given to an act named only inside another act's quoted title; and an act whose vigencia clause has a general rule and exceptions is dated by the rule that covers the changed provision (Ley 890 de 2004: arts. 7 to 13 immediately, the rest from 1 January 2005; Ley 2502 de 2025: art. 296 of the Código Penal one year after promulgation; Ley 2080 de 2021: from publication).
- The source's earlier texts often leave out the article's epigraph (its title); where the latest earlier text differs from today's only by that or by a change no note names, today's text is used from the latest named act's day. - Fallback audit (2026-10-31), summary: each of the 42 articles listed above as taking today's text from the latest named act's day was compared with the source's earlier text for that act; 14 of them had later words in an earlier version and now carry the source's earlier texts until the act or ruling that changed them (the decisions headed 'Fallback audit (2026-10-31)'); in the other 28 the difference is only the heading line, the source's punctuation or a slip in its transcription, or today's text does apply from that day, or the engine's later steps replaced that text, so no earlier version holds later words.
- Ley 617 de 2000, art. 33: the page prints its last words under '<Derogado por el artículo 154 ... de la Ley 2200 de 2022>'; they are the article's text from the act's first day (numeral 5 with 'segundo grado de consanguinidad', which Sentencia C-325 de 2009 of 13 May 2009 replaced with 'tercer grado de consanguinidad', as the page shows), and the article is gone from Ley 2200 de 2022's day.
- Sistema General de Participaciones (Ley 715 de 2001): article words the page puts under anchors named like headings ('Nivel001', 'TITULO I', ...) had been read as headings, so they were missing from their articles (in titulos.md, or nowhere); they are back in them: art. 84A (5 blocks under anchors named 'TITULO I').
- Protección de la competencia (Ley 1340 de 2009), Competencia desleal (Ley 256 de 1996), Tecnologías de la Información y las Comunicaciones (Ley 1341 de 2009), Ley Estatutaria de Salud (Ley 1751 de 2015), Reforma del Sistema General de Seguridad Social en Salud (Ley 1438 de 2011), Violencia y discriminación contra las mujeres (Ley 1257 de 2008), Acoso laboral (Ley 1010 de 2006), Sistema de Riesgos Laborales (Ley 1562 de 2012), Garantías mobiliarias (Ley 1676 de 2013), Sistema Nacional de Reforma Agraria y Desarrollo Rural Campesino (Ley 160 de 1994): versions are rebuilt per article from the basedoc's earlier texts, inline amendment markers and Notas de Vigencia, as for the Constitution and the Código Civil. Where the source names an act but gives no text after it, the article reads '[La fuente no da el texto vigente en esta fecha.]' for that period.
- Constitutional Court rulings that struck text are laws of kind 'court', dated by the ruling's day as the source gives it; rulings that upheld text on a condition are court laws with a note and change no words.
- In-force days of amending acts: the act's own vigencia clause as its basedoc page gives it; otherwise the default rule (Ley 4 de 1913, art. 52: two months after promulgation). A day a provision's own note gives applies to that provision. The list below gives the day and the rule for each act.
- Dates come only from the act itself: the Diario Oficial day on the act's own page wins over a day the code's notes give; a note's Diario Oficial day is not given to an act named only inside another act's quoted title; and an act whose vigencia clause has a general rule and exceptions is dated by the rule that covers the changed provision (Ley 890 de 2004: arts. 7 to 13 immediately, the rest from 1 January 2005; Ley 2502 de 2025: art. 296 of the Código Penal one year after promulgation; Ley 2080 de 2021: from publication).
- The source's earlier texts often leave out the article's epigraph (its title); where the latest earlier text differs from today's only by that or by a change no note names, today's text is used from the latest named act's day. Articles where that happened: ley_0160_1994 art-0011, ley_0160_1994 art-0012, ley_0160_1994 art-0078, ley_0160_1994 art-0107, ley_1341_2009 art-0018, ley_1341_2009 art-0022, ley_1341_2009 art-0037, ley_1341_2009 art-0069, ley_1438_2011 art-0050, ley_1438_2011 art-0100, ley_1438_2011 art-0103.

- Fallback audit (2026-10-31), summary: each of the 11 articles listed above as taking today's text from the latest named act's day was compared with the source's earlier text for that act; 6 of them had later words in an earlier version and now carry the source's earlier texts until the act or ruling that changed them (the decisions headed 'Fallback audit (2026-10-31)'); in the other 5 the difference is only the heading line, the source's punctuation or a slip in its transcription, or today's text does apply from that day, or the engine's later steps replaced that text, so no earlier version holds later words; except, left as they are because the source does not settle them: Art. 107 (the committee's composition (source's original text) left the page with Resolución Minagricultura 183 de 2024, an administrative act with no day given; the published versions lack the list from 1994).

Where the source names an act but gives no text after it, the article reads '[La fuente no da el texto vigente en esta fecha.]' for that period.
- Constitutional Court rulings that struck text are laws of kind 'court', dated by the ruling's day as the source gives it; rulings that upheld text on a condition are court laws with a note and change no words.
- In-force days of amending acts: the act's own vigencia clause as its basedoc page gives it; otherwise the default rule (Ley 4 de 1913, art. 52: two months after promulgation). A day a provision's own note gives applies to that provision. The list below gives the day and the rule for each act.
- Dates come only from the act itself: the Diario Oficial day on the act's own page wins over a day the code's notes give; a note's Diario Oficial day is not given to an act named only inside another act's quoted title; and an act whose vigencia clause has a general rule and exceptions is dated by the rule that covers the changed provision (Ley 890 de 2004: arts. 7 to 13 immediately, the rest from 1 January 2005; Ley 2502 de 2025: art. 296 of the Código Penal one year after promulgation; Ley 2080 de 2021: from publication).
- The source's earlier texts often leave out the article's epigraph (its title); where the latest earlier text differs from today's only by that or by a change no note names, today's text is used from the latest named act's day. - Fallback audit (2026-10-31), summary: each of the 5 articles listed above as taking today's text from the latest named act's day was compared with the source's earlier text for that act; 2 of them had later words in an earlier version and now carry the source's earlier texts until the act or ruling that changed them (the decisions headed 'Fallback audit (2026-10-31)'); in the other 3 the difference is only the heading line, the source's punctuation or a slip in its transcription, or today's text does apply from that day, or the engine's later steps replaced that text, so no earlier version holds later words.
- Violencia intrafamiliar (Ley 294 de 1996), Prevención de la violencia sexual contra niños, niñas y adolescentes (Ley 1146 de 2007), Derechos de los adultos mayores (Ley 1251 de 2008), Ley de Protección Integral a la Familia (Ley 1361 de 2009), Estatuto de Ciudadanía Juvenil (Ley 1622 de 2013), Tecnologías de la información en las actuaciones judiciales (Ley 2213 de 2022), Normalización de la cartera pública (Ley 1066 de 2006), Modernización de la organización y el funcionamiento de los municipios (Ley 1551 de 2012), Régimen de las Áreas Metropolitanas (Ley 1625 de 2013), Régimen Departamental (Ley 2200 de 2022): versions are rebuilt per article from the basedoc's earlier texts, inline amendment markers and Notas de Vigencia, as for the Constitution and the Código Civil. Where the source names an act but gives no text after it, the article reads '[La fuente no da el texto vigente en esta fecha.]' for that period.
- Constitutional Court rulings that struck text are laws of kind 'court', dated by the ruling's day as the source gives it; rulings that upheld text on a condition are court laws with a note and change no words.
- In-force days of amending acts: the act's own vigencia clause as its basedoc page gives it; otherwise the default rule (Ley 4 de 1913, art. 52: two months after promulgation). A day a provision's own note gives applies to that provision. The list below gives the day and the rule for each act.
- Dates come only from the act itself: the Diario Oficial day on the act's own page wins over a day the code's notes give; a note's Diario Oficial day is not given to an act named only inside another act's quoted title; and an act whose vigencia clause has a general rule and exceptions is dated by the rule that covers the changed provision (Ley 890 de 2004: arts. 7 to 13 immediately, the rest from 1 January 2005; Ley 2502 de 2025: art. 296 of the Código Penal one year after promulgation; Ley 2080 de 2021: from publication).
- The source's earlier texts often leave out the article's epigraph (its title); where the latest earlier text differs from today's only by that or by a change no note names, today's text is used from the latest named act's day. Articles where that happened: ley_1251_2008 art-0007, ley_1551_2012 art-0035.

- Fallback audit (2026-10-31), summary: each of the 2 articles listed above as taking today's text from the latest named act's day was compared with the source's earlier text for that act; 1 of them had later words in an earlier version and now carry the source's earlier texts until the act or ruling that changed them (the decisions headed 'Fallback audit (2026-10-31)'); in the other 1 the difference is only the heading line, the source's punctuation or a slip in its transcription, or today's text does apply from that day, or the engine's later steps replaced that text, so no earlier version holds later words.
- Régimen de Propiedad Horizontal (Ley 675 de 2001), Arrendamiento de vivienda urbana (Ley 820 de 2003), Estatuto de Registro de Instrumentos Públicos (Ley 1579 de 2012), Financiación de vivienda (Ley 546 de 1999), Desarrollo urbano y acceso a la vivienda (Ley 1537 de 2012), Ley Eléctrica (Ley 143 de 1994), Energías renovables no convencionales (Ley 1715 de 2014), Gestión del cambio climático (Ley 1931 de 2018), Protección de personas con discapacidad mental (Ley 1306 de 2009): versions are rebuilt per article from the basedoc's earlier texts, inline amendment markers and Notas de Vigencia, as for the Constitution and the Código Civil. Where the source names an act but gives no text after it, the article reads '[La fuente no da el texto vigente en esta fecha.]' for that period.
- Constitutional Court rulings that struck text are laws of kind 'court', dated by the ruling's day as the source gives it; rulings that upheld text on a condition are court laws with a note and change no words.
- In-force days of amending acts: the act's own vigencia clause as its basedoc page gives it; otherwise the default rule (Ley 4 de 1913, art. 52: two months after promulgation). A day a provision's own note gives applies to that provision. The list below gives the day and the rule for each act.
- Dates come only from the act itself: the Diario Oficial day on the act's own page wins over a day the code's notes give; a note's Diario Oficial day is not given to an act named only inside another act's quoted title; and an act whose vigencia clause has a general rule and exceptions is dated by the rule that covers the changed provision (Ley 890 de 2004: arts. 7 to 13 immediately, the rest from 1 January 2005; Ley 2502 de 2025: art. 296 of the Código Penal one year after promulgation; Ley 2080 de 2021: from publication).
- The source's earlier texts often leave out the article's epigraph (its title); where the latest earlier text differs from today's only by that or by a change no note names, today's text is used from the latest named act's day. Articles where that happened: ley_0143_1994 art-0011.

- Fallback audit (2026-10-31), summary: each of the 1 articles listed above as taking today's text from the latest named act's day was compared with the source's earlier text for that act; 1 of them had later words in an earlier version and now carry the source's earlier texts until the act or ruling that changed them (the decisions headed 'Fallback audit (2026-10-31)'); in the other 0 the difference is only the heading line, the source's punctuation or a slip in its transcription, or today's text does apply from that day, or the engine's later steps replaced that text, so no earlier version holds later words.
- Ley de Transporte (Ley 105 de 1993), Estatuto Nacional de Transporte (Ley 336 de 1996), Código Sanitario Nacional (Ley 9 de 1979), Código de Régimen Municipal (Decreto 1333 de 1986): versions are rebuilt per article from the basedoc's earlier texts, inline amendment markers and Notas de Vigencia, as for the Constitution and the Código Civil. Where the source names an act but gives no text after it, the article reads '[La fuente no da el texto vigente en esta fecha.]' for that period.
- Constitutional Court rulings that struck text are laws of kind 'court', dated by the ruling's day as the source gives it; rulings that upheld text on a condition are court laws with a note and change no words.
- In-force days of amending acts: the act's own vigencia clause as its basedoc page gives it; otherwise the default rule (Ley 4 de 1913, art. 52: two months after promulgation). A day a provision's own note gives applies to that provision. The list below gives the day and the rule for each act.
- Dates come only from the act itself: the Diario Oficial day on the act's own page wins over a day the code's notes give; a note's Diario Oficial day is not given to an act named only inside another act's quoted title; and an act whose vigencia clause has a general rule and exceptions is dated by the rule that covers the changed provision (Ley 890 de 2004: arts. 7 to 13 immediately, the rest from 1 January 2005; Ley 2502 de 2025: art. 296 of the Código Penal one year after promulgation; Ley 2080 de 2021: from publication).
- The source's earlier texts often leave out the article's epigraph (its title); where the latest earlier text differs from today's only by that or by a change no note names, today's text is used from the latest named act's day.
