- Fallback audit (2026-10-31), summary: each of the 19 articles listed above as taking today's text from the latest named act's day was compared with the source's earlier text for that act; 10 of them had later words in an earlier version and now carry the source's earlier texts until the act or ruling that changed them (the decisions headed 'Fallback audit (2026-10-31)'); in the other 9 the difference is only the heading line, the source's punctuation or a slip in its transcription, or today's text does apply from that day, or the engine's later steps replaced that text, so no earlier version holds later words; except, left as they are because the source does not settle them: Art. 137 (the page prints, under art. 137 (repealed by Ley 136 de 1994 art. 173), the text of Ley 136 art. 173; the part's today's text is that printed text, so a fix changes today's text); Art. 53 (the page prints the original text of an article repealed in part by Ley 383 de 1997 and Ley 443 de 1998 ('El texto original de este artículo es el siguiente'); the part's today's text is that printed text (printed-repeal case)).
- Estatuto Básico de los partidos y movimientos políticos (Ley 130 de 1994), Organización de los partidos y movimientos políticos y de los procesos electorales (Ley 1475 de 2011), Ley de Garantías Electorales (Ley 996 de 2005), Disposiciones en materia electoral (Ley 163 de 1994), Estatuto de la Oposición Política (Ley 1909 de 2018), Veedurías ciudadanas (Ley 850 de 2003), Micro, pequeñas y medianas empresas (Ley 590 de 2000), Ley de Emprendimiento (Ley 2069 de 2020), Fomento a la cultura del emprendimiento (Ley 1014 de 2006), Régimen salarial y prestacional de los empleados públicos (Ley 4 de 1992): versions are rebuilt per article from the basedoc's earlier texts, inline amendment markers and Notas de Vigencia, as for the Constitution and the Código Civil. Where the source names an act but gives no text after it, the article reads '[La fuente no da el texto vigente en esta fecha.]' for that period.
- Constitutional Court rulings that struck text are laws of kind 'court', dated by the ruling's day as the source gives it; rulings that upheld text on a condition are court laws with a note and change no words.
- In-force days of amending acts: the act's own vigencia clause as its basedoc page gives it; otherwise the default rule (Ley 4 de 1913, art. 52: two months after promulgation). A day a provision's own note gives applies to that provision. The list below gives the day and the rule for each act.
- Dates come only from the act itself: the Diario Oficial day on the act's own page wins over a day the code's notes give; a note's Diario Oficial day is not given to an act named only inside another act's quoted title; and an act whose vigencia clause has a general rule and exceptions is dated by the rule that covers the changed provision (Ley 890 de 2004: arts. 7 to 13 immediately, the rest from 1 January 2005; Ley 2502 de 2025: art. 296 of the Código Penal one year after promulgation; Ley 2080 de 2021: from publication).
- The source's earlier texts often leave out the article's epigraph (its title); where the latest earlier text differs from today's only by that or by a change no note names, today's text is used from the latest named act's day. Articles where that happened: ley_0130_1994 art-0039, ley_0590_2000 art-0002, ley_0590_2000 art-0023.

- Fallback audit (2026-10-31), summary: each of the 3 articles listed above as taking today's text from the latest named act's day was compared with the source's earlier text for that act; 1 of them had later words in an earlier version and now carry the source's earlier texts until the act or ruling that changed them (the decisions headed 'Fallback audit (2026-10-31)'); in the other 2 the difference is only the heading line, the source's punctuation or a slip in its transcription, or today's text does apply from that day, or the engine's later steps replaced that text, so no earlier version holds later words.
- Sistema General de Regalías (Ley 2056 de 2020), Integración social de las personas con limitación (Ley 361 de 1997), Teletrabajo (Ley 1221 de 2008), Trabajo en casa (Ley 2088 de 2021), Trabajo remoto (Ley 2121 de 2021), Ley de Salud Mental (Ley 1616 de 2013), Transparencia, prevención y lucha contra la corrupción (Ley 2195 de 2022), Racionalización de trámites (Ley 2052 de 2020), Sanciones para condenados por corrupción (Ley 2014 de 2019), Sistema Nacional de Discapacidad (Ley 1145 de 2007): versions are rebuilt per article from the basedoc's earlier texts, inline amendment markers and Notas de Vigencia, as for the Constitution and the Código Civil. Where the source names an act but gives no text after it, the article reads '[La fuente no da el texto vigente en esta fecha.]' for that period.
- Constitutional Court rulings that struck text are laws of kind 'court', dated by the ruling's day as the source gives it; rulings that upheld text on a condition are court laws with a note and change no words.
- In-force days of amending acts: the act's own vigencia clause as its basedoc page gives it; otherwise the default rule (Ley 4 de 1913, art. 52: two months after promulgation). A day a provision's own note gives applies to that provision. The list below gives the day and the rule for each act.
- Dates come only from the act itself: the Diario Oficial day on the act's own page wins over a day the code's notes give; a note's Diario Oficial day is not given to an act named only inside another act's quoted title; and an act whose vigencia clause has a general rule and exceptions is dated by the rule that covers the changed provision (Ley 890 de 2004: arts. 7 to 13 immediately, the rest from 1 January 2005; Ley 2502 de 2025: art. 296 of the Código Penal one year after promulgation; Ley 2080 de 2021: from publication).
- The source's earlier texts often leave out the article's epigraph (its title); where the latest earlier text differs from today's only by that or by a change no note names, today's text is used from the latest named act's day. Articles where that happened: ley_1616_2013 art-0005, ley_2014_2019 art-0006, ley_2195_2022 art-0065.

- Fallback audit (2026-10-31), summary: each of the 5 articles listed above as taking today's text from the latest named act's day was compared with the source's earlier text for that act; 3 of them had later words in an earlier version and now carry the source's earlier texts until the act or ruling that changed them (the decisions headed 'Fallback audit (2026-10-31)'); in the other 2 the difference is only the heading line, the source's punctuation or a slip in its transcription, or today's text does apply from that day, or the engine's later steps replaced that text, so no earlier version holds later words; except, left as they are because the source does not settle them: Art. 53 (the page prints the text of an article repealed by Decreto 2478 de 1999 art. 22 (no ruling against that decree is noted); the part's today's text is that printed text (printed-repeal case)).

- Conglomerados financieros (Ley 1870 de 2017): Art. 2 — words the page gives after a heading anchor (a quoted chapter, or a title anchor misplaced inside the article's heading) had been read as headings and were missing from the article; they are back in it.
- Código Penal Militar (Ley 1407 de 2010), Código Disciplinario Militar (Ley 1862 de 2017), Régimen Disciplinario para la Policía Nacional (Ley 1015 de 2006), Hábeas corpus (Ley 1095 de 2006), Estados de Excepción (Ley 137 de 1994), Inteligencia y contrainteligencia (Ley 1621 de 2013), Orden público y convivencia (Ley 418 de 1997), Ley de Justicia y Paz (Ley 975 de 2005), Estatutaria de la Administración de Justicia en la Jurisdicción Especial para la Paz (Ley 1957 de 2019), Reglas de procedimiento de la Jurisdicción Especial para la Paz (Ley 1922 de 2018): versions are rebuilt per article from the basedoc's earlier texts, inline amendment markers and Notas de Vigencia, as for the Constitution and the Código Civil. Where the source names an act but gives no text after it, the article reads '[La fuente no da el texto vigente en esta fecha.]' for that period.
- Constitutional Court rulings that struck text are laws of kind 'court', dated by the ruling's day as the source gives it; rulings that upheld text on a condition are court laws with a note and change no words.
- In-force days of amending acts: the act's own vigencia clause as its basedoc page gives it; otherwise the default rule (Ley 4 de 1913, art. 52: two months after promulgation). A day a provision's own note gives applies to that provision. The list below gives the day and the rule for each act.
- Dates come only from the act itself: the Diario Oficial day on the act's own page wins over a day the code's notes give; a note's Diario Oficial day is not given to an act named only inside another act's quoted title; and an act whose vigencia clause has a general rule and exceptions is dated by the rule that covers the changed provision (Ley 890 de 2004: arts. 7 to 13 immediately, the rest from 1 January 2005; Ley 2502 de 2025: art. 296 of the Código Penal one year after promulgation; Ley 2080 de 2021: from publication).
- The source's earlier texts often leave out the article's epigraph (its title); where the latest earlier text differs from today's only by that or by a change no note names, today's text is used from the latest named act's day. Articles where that happened: ley_0418_1997 art-0006, ley_0975_2005 art-0048, ley_1015_2006 art-0037, ley_1015_2006 art-0054.

- Fallback audit (2026-10-31), summary: each of the 4 articles listed above as taking today's text from the latest named act's day was compared with the source's earlier text for that act; 1 of them had later words in an earlier version and now carry the source's earlier texts until the act or ruling that changed them (the decisions headed 'Fallback audit (2026-10-31)'); in the other 3 the difference is only the heading line, the source's punctuation or a slip in its transcription, or today's text does apply from that day, or the engine's later steps replaced that text, so no earlier version holds later words.
- Evaluación de la calidad de la educación (Ley 1324 de 2009), Registro calificado de programas de educación superior (Ley 1188 de 2008), Educación superior técnica profesional y tecnológica (Ley 749 de 2002), Ciencia, tecnología e innovación (Ley 1286 de 2009), Ministerio de Ciencia, Tecnología e Innovación (Ley 1951 de 2019), Inspección y vigilancia de la educación superior (Ley 1740 de 2014), Sistema Nacional de Convivencia Escolar (Ley 1620 de 2013), Educación para el trabajo y el desarrollo humano (Ley 1064 de 2006), Espectáculos públicos de las artes escénicas (Ley 1493 de 2011), Economía creativa, Ley Naranja (Ley 1834 de 2017): versions are rebuilt per article from the basedoc's earlier texts, inline amendment markers and Notas de Vigencia, as for the Constitution and the Código Civil. Where the source names an act but gives no text after it, the article reads '[La fuente no da el texto vigente en esta fecha.]' for that period.
- Constitutional Court rulings that struck text are laws of kind 'court', dated by the ruling's day as the source gives it; rulings that upheld text on a condition are court laws with a note and change no words.
- In-force days of amending acts: the act's own vigencia clause as its basedoc page gives it; otherwise the default rule (Ley 4 de 1913, art. 52: two months after promulgation). A day a provision's own note gives applies to that provision. The list below gives the day and the rule for each act.
- Dates come only from the act itself: the Diario Oficial day on the act's own page wins over a day the code's notes give; a note's Diario Oficial day is not given to an act named only inside another act's quoted title; and an act whose vigencia clause has a general rule and exceptions is dated by the rule that covers the changed provision (Ley 890 de 2004: arts. 7 to 13 immediately, the rest from 1 January 2005; Ley 2502 de 2025: art. 296 of the Código Penal one year after promulgation; Ley 2080 de 2021: from publication).
- The source's earlier texts often leave out the article's epigraph (its title); where the latest earlier text differs from today's only by that or by a change no note names, today's text is used from the latest named act's day. Articles where that happened: ley_1286_2009 art-0008, ley_1324_2009 art-0010.

- In-force days of amending acts: the act's own vigencia clause as its basedoc page gives it; otherwise the default rule (Ley 4 de 1913, art. 52: two months after promulgation). A day a provision's own note gives applies to that provision. The list below gives the day and the rule for each act.
- Dates come only from the act itself: the Diario Oficial day on the act's own page wins over a day the code's notes give; a note's Diario Oficial day is not given to an act named only inside another act's quoted title; and an act whose vigencia clause has a general rule and exceptions is dated by the rule that covers the changed provision (Ley 890 de 2004: arts. 7 to 13 immediately, the rest from 1 January 2005; Ley 2502 de 2025: art. 296 of the Código Penal one year after promulgation; Ley 2080 de 2021: from publication).
- The source's earlier texts often leave out the article's epigraph (its title); where the latest earlier text differs from today's only by that or by a change no note names, today's text is used from the latest named act's day. Articles where that happened: ley_1733_2014 art-0007.

- Moralidad en la administración pública y lucha contra la corrupción (Ley 190 de 1995), Control interno de las entidades del Estado (Ley 87 de 1993), Amnistía, indulto y tratamientos penales especiales (Ley 1820 de 2016), Principios y normas de contabilidad e información financiera (Ley 1314 de 2009), Ejercicio de la ingeniería y Código de Ética Profesional (Ley 842 de 2003), Mecanismo de protección al cesante (Ley 1636 de 2013), Derecho de autor, reforma de la Ley 23 de 1982 (Ley 44 de 1993), Construcciones sismo resistentes (Ley 400 de 1997), Libranza o descuento directo (Ley 1527 de 2012), Código de Ética y Disciplinario del Congresista (Ley 1828 de 2017): versions are rebuilt per article from the basedoc's earlier texts, inline amendment markers and Notas de Vigencia, as for the Constitution and the Código Civil. Where the source names an act but gives no text after it, the article reads '[La fuente no da el texto vigente en esta fecha.]' for that period.
- Constitutional Court rulings that struck text are laws of kind 'court', dated by the ruling's day as the source gives it; rulings that upheld text on a condition are court laws with a note and change no words.
- In-force days of amending acts: the act's own vigencia clause as its basedoc page gives it; otherwise the default rule (Ley 4 de 1913, art. 52: two months after promulgation). A day a provision's own note gives applies to that provision. The list below gives the day and the rule for each act.
- Dates come only from the act itself: the Diario Oficial day on the act's own page wins over a day the code's notes give; a note's Diario Oficial day is not given to an act named only inside another act's quoted title; and an act whose vigencia clause has a general rule and exceptions is dated by the rule that covers the changed provision (Ley 890 de 2004: arts. 7 to 13 immediately, the rest from 1 January 2005; Ley 2502 de 2025: art. 296 of the Código Penal one year after promulgation; Ley 2080 de 2021: from publication).
- The source's earlier texts often leave out the article's epigraph (its title); where the latest earlier text differs from today's only by that or by a change no note names, today's text is used from the latest named act's day. Articles where that happened: none.

(the day after its publication in the Diario Oficial of 2016-12-30)

- Derecho de autor, reforma de la Ley 23 de 1982 (Ley 44 de 1993): first version from 1993-02-05: Ley 44 de 1993, art. 70: 'Derógase el artículo 174 de la Ley 23/82. La presente Ley rige a partir de su publicación en el "Diario Oficial".' (sanctioned 1993-02-05, Diario Oficial of 1993-02-05)
- Construcciones sismo resistentes (Ley 400 de 1997): first version from 1998-02-19: Ley 400 de 1997, art. 56: 'La presente ley rige a partir de los seis (6) meses siguientes a la fecha de su sanción. Quienes soliciten licencias de construcción durante ese período, podrán acogerse a sus requisitos.' (six months after its sanction of 1997-08-19; Diario Oficial of 1997-08-25)
- Libranza o descuento directo (Ley 1527 de 2012): first version from 2012-04-27: Ley 1527 de 2012, art. 23: 'Las disposiciones contenidas en los artículos 6o y 8o de la presente ley entrarán a regir seis meses después de su promulgación. Las personas naturales o jurídicas que a la fecha de entrada en vigencia de la presente ley estén llevando a cabo operaciones de enajenación de derechos patrimoniales de contenido crediticio derivados de operaciones de libranza en términos contrarios a los establecidos en el artículo 6o, deberán tomar las medidas necesarias para dar cumplimiento a dichas disposiciones antes de su entrada en vigencia. En caso contrario deberán acordar con la Superintendencia de Sociedades un plan de desmonte progresivo de sus actividades. Las demás disposiciones de la presente ley rigen a partir de la fecha de su promulgación y derogan todas las disposiciones que le sean contrarias.' The first version is dated from promulgation (2012-04-27), when all but arts. 6 and 8 took effect; those two six months later (2012-10-27).
- Código de Ética y Disciplinario del Congresista (Ley 1828 de 2017): first version from 2017-01-23: Ley 1828 de 2017, art. 73: 'La presente ley rige a partir de la fecha de su publicación y deroga todas las disposiciones que le sean contrarias.' (sanctioned 2017-01-23, Diario Oficial of 2017-01-23)
- Moralidad en la administración pública y lucha contra la corrupción (Ley 190 de 1995): the page prints each Código Penal or Código de Procedimiento Penal article the act rewrites under an anchor of its own, after the act's article that says 'quedará así:'; each is read as part of that article, with its own heading: art. 63 into art. 18; art. 133 into art. 19; art. 138 into art. 20; art. 140 into art. 21; art. 141 into art. 22; art. 142 into art. 23; art. 143 into art. 24; art. 147 into art. 25; art. 149 into art. 28; art. 150 into art. 29; art. 151 into art. 30; art. 177 into art. 31; art. 569 into art. 34. art. 1: the earlier text of numeral 5 is printed under the article's heading ('ARTÍCULO 1o.'), which made it read as the whole article; it is read as numeral 5's; art. 2: its original text, which Ley 443 de 1998 replaced as a whole, is printed without the article's heading; it is read as the whole article; art. 31: the marker '<Modificado por el artículo 7o. de la Ley 365 de 1997 ...>' stands on its own paragraph after the article's first one; it is read as the article's marker.
- Amnistía, indulto y tratamientos penales especiales (Ley 1820 de 2016): Ley 1820 de 2016, art. 23: Sentencia C-007-18 (1 March 2018) upheld the article except the words 'únicamente' and 'graves' of its parágrafo and the parágrafo's last sentence ('Se entenderá por "grave crimen de guerra" ...'), which it struck; the page shows them struck (the sentence's final full stop is printed outside the struck words, so today's text keeps it), and the ruling as one that upheld the article. History: the words are in the text from the act's first day to the ruling's day.
- Ejercicio de la ingeniería y Código de Ética Profesional (Ley 842 de 2003): Ley 842 de 2003, art. 78: the page prints the text the Court's Auto A-226-03 (2 December 2003) set ('<Artículo corregido en los términos del Auto A-226-03>', without 'la Ley 211 de 1995'), the original text in its earlier-text box, and the parágrafo struck by Sentencia C-570-04 (8 June 2004). History: the original text from the act's first day; the corrected inciso from the Auto's day; the parágrafo out from the ruling's day.
- Derecho de autor, reforma de la Ley 23 de 1982 (Ley 44 de 1993): Ley 44 de 1993, arts. 14 (numeral 5) and 21 (incisos 1 and 3): Ley 719 de 2001 (in force 29 December 2001) rewrote them, and Sentencia C-975-02 (13 November 2002) struck Ley 719 de 2001 as a whole, so the act's own words, which the page prints as today's text, were in force again. History: today's words from the act's first day; the Ley 719 wording the earlier-text boxes give ('Texto modificado por la Ley 719 de 2001') from 29 December 2001; today's words again from the ruling's day.
- Moralidad en la administración pública y lucha contra la corrupción (Ley 190 de 1995) and the other acts of batch 23: Ley 1796 de 2016 (art. 36: 'La presente ley entrará a regir a partir de su promulgación, con excepción del Título IV, el cual entrará a regir un (1) año después de su promulgación'): the changes its arts. 3, 4, 5, 15, 16, 17, 36 made (outside Título IV, arts. 20 to 31) are dated from its promulgation, 13 July 2016.

- Código del Menor (Decreto 2737 de 1989): versions are rebuilt per article from the basedoc's earlier texts, inline amendment markers and Notas de Vigencia, as for the Constitution and the Código Civil. Where the source names an act but gives no text after it, the article reads '[La fuente no da el texto vigente en esta fecha.]' for that period.
- Constitutional Court rulings that struck text are laws of kind 'court', dated by the ruling's day as the source gives it; rulings that upheld text on a condition are court laws with a note and change no words.
- In-force days of amending acts: the act's own vigencia clause as its basedoc page gives it; otherwise the default rule (Ley 4 de 1913, art. 52: two months after promulgation). A day a provision's own note gives applies to that provision. The list below gives the day and the rule for each act.
- Dates come only from the act itself: the Diario Oficial day on the act's own page wins over a day the code's notes give; a note's Diario Oficial day is not given to an act named only inside another act's quoted title; and an act whose vigencia clause has a general rule and exceptions is dated by the rule that covers the changed provision (Ley 890 de 2004: arts. 7 to 13 immediately, the rest from 1 January 2005; Ley 2502 de 2025: art. 296 of the Código Penal one year after promulgation; Ley 2080 de 2021: from publication).
- The source's earlier texts often leave out the article's epigraph (its title); where the latest earlier text differs from today's only by that or by a change no note names, today's text is used from the latest named act's day. Articles where that happened: none.

- In-force days of amending acts: the act's own vigencia clause as its basedoc page gives it; otherwise the default rule (Ley 4 de 1913, art. 52: two months after promulgation). A day a provision's own note gives applies to that provision. The list below gives the day and the rule for each act.
- Dates come only from the act itself: the Diario Oficial day on the act's own page wins over a day the code's notes give; a note's Diario Oficial day is not given to an act named only inside another act's quoted title; and an act whose vigencia clause has a general rule and exceptions is dated by the rule that covers the changed provision (Ley 890 de 2004: arts. 7 to 13 immediately, the rest from 1 January 2005; Ley 2502 de 2025: art. 296 of the Código Penal one year after promulgation; Ley 2080 de 2021: from publication).

- In-force days of amending acts: the act's own vigencia clause as its basedoc page gives it; otherwise the default rule (Ley 4 de 1913, art. 52: two months after promulgation). A day a provision's own note gives applies to that provision. The list below gives the day and the rule for each act.
- Dates come only from the act itself: the Diario Oficial day on the act's own page wins over a day the code's notes give; a note's Diario Oficial day is not given to an act named only inside another act's quoted title; and an act whose vigencia clause has a general rule and exceptions is dated by the rule that covers the changed provision (Ley 890 de 2004: arts. 7 to 13 immediately, the rest from 1 January 2005; Ley 2502 de 2025: art. 296 of the Código Penal one year after promulgation; Ley 2080 de 2021: from publication).
- The source's earlier texts often leave out the article's epigraph (its title); where the latest earlier text differs from today's only by that or by a change no note names, today's text is used from the latest named act's day. Articles where that happened: none.

- Constitutional Court rulings that struck text are laws of kind 'court', dated by the ruling's day as the source gives it; rulings that upheld text on a condition are court laws with a note and change no words.
- In-force days of amending acts: the act's own vigencia clause as its basedoc page gives it; otherwise the default rule (Ley 4 de 1913, art. 52: two months after promulgation). A day a provision's own note gives applies to that provision. The list below gives the day and the rule for each act.
- Dates come only from the act itself: the Diario Oficial day on the act's own page wins over a day the code's notes give; a note's Diario Oficial day is not given to an act named only inside another act's quoted title; and an act whose vigencia clause has a general rule and exceptions is dated by the rule that covers the changed provision (Ley 890 de 2004: arts. 7 to 13 immediately, the rest from 1 January 2005; Ley 2502 de 2025: art. 296 of the Código Penal one year after promulgation; Ley 2080 de 2021: from publication).
- The source's earlier texts often leave out the article's epigraph (its title); where the latest earlier text differs from today's only by that or by a change no note names, today's text is used from the latest named act's day. Articles where that happened: decreto_1295_1994 art-0006.

- The source's earlier texts often leave out the article's epigraph (its title); where the latest earlier text differs from today's only by that or by a change no note names, today's text is used from the latest named act's day. Articles where that happened: none.

- In force 2010-07-12: Ley 1393 de 2010 (Ley 1393 de 2010, art. 41: 'La presente ley rige a partir de la fecha de su publicación y deroga las disposiciones que le sean contrarias, en especial el artículo 2o del Decreto 1289 de 2010.' (sanctioned 2010-07-12, Diario Oficial of 2010-07-12)).
- In force 2011-01-06: Decreto 17 de 2011 (Decreto 17 de 2011: 'ARTÍCULO 19. VIGENCIA. El presente decreto rige a partir de la fecha de su publicación.').
- In force 2011-11-03: Decreto 4142 de 2011 (Decreto 4142 de 2011: 'ARTÍCULO 26. VIGENCIA. El presente decreto rige a partir de la fecha de su publicación.').
- In force 2012-01-10: Decreto 19 de 2012 (Decreto 19 de 2012: 'ARTÍCULO 238. VIGENCIA. El presente Decreto-ley rige a partir de la fecha de su publicación.').
- In force 2016-07-13: Ley 1797 de 2016 (Ley 1797 de 2016, art. 28: 'La presente ley rige a partir de su publicación y deroga todas las normas que le sean contrarias.' (sanctioned 2016-07-13, Diario Oficial of 2016-07-13)).
- In force 2016-12-29: Ley 1819 de 2016 (Ley 1819 de 2016: 'ARTÍCULO 376. VIGENCIAS Y DEROGATORIAS. La presente ley rige a partir de su promulgación, deroga todas las disposiciones que le sean contrarias y en especial las siguientes:').
- In force 2017-12-20: Ley 1873 de 2017 (Ley 1873 de 2017: 'ARTÍCULO 144. La presente ley rige a partir de la fecha de su publicación y surte efectos fiscales a partir del 1 de enero de 2018.').
- In force 2018-07-12: Ley 1917 de 2018 (Ley 1917 de 2018: 'ARTÍCULO 16. VIGENCIA Y DEROGATORIAS. La presente ley empezará a regir a partir de la fecha de su publicación y deroga las disposiciones que le sean contrarias.').
- In force 2018-11-26: Ley 1940 de 2018 (Ley 1940 de 2018: 'ARTÍCULO 152. La presente ley rige a partir de la fecha de su publicación y surte efectos fiscales a partir del 1 de enero de 2019.').
- In force 2019-05-25: Ley 1955 de 2019 (Ley 1955 de 2019: 'ARTÍCULO 336. VIGENCIAS Y DEROGATORIAS. La presente ley rige a partir de su publicación y deroga todas las disposiciones que le sean contrarias. Los artículos de las Leyes 812 de 2003, 1151 de 2007, 1450 de 2011, y 1753 de 2015 no derogados expresamente en el siguiente Inciso o por otras leyes conti').
- In force 2019-12-27: Ley 2008 de 2019 (Ley 2008 de 2019: 'ARTÍCULO 155. La presente ley rige a partir de la fecha de su publicación y surte efectos fiscales a partir del 1 de enero de 2020.').
- In force 2020-04-12: Decreto 538 de 2020 (Decreto 538 de 2020: 'ARTÍCULO 29. VIGENCIA. El presente derecho rige a partir de la fecha de su publicación.').
- In force 2020-04-15: Decreto 576 de 2020 (Decreto 576 de 2020: 'ARTÍCULO 10. VIGENCIA. El presente Decreto Legislativo rige a partir de la fecha de su publicación.').
- In force 2020-06-04: Decreto 800 de 2020 (Decreto 800 de 2020: 'ARTÍCULO 11. VIGENCIA. El presente derecho legislativo rige a partir de la fecha de su publicación.').
- In force 2023-05-19: Ley 2294 de 2023 (Ley 2294 de 2023, art. 372: 'La presente ley rige a partir de su publicación y deroga todas las disposiciones que le sean contrarias. Los artículos de las Leyes 812 de 2003, 1151 de 2007, 1450 de 2011, 1753 de 2015 y 1955 de 2019 no derogados expresamente en el siguiente inciso o por otras leyes, continuarán vigentes hasta que sean derogados o modificados por norma posterior. Se derogan expresamente el artículo 26 de la Ley 45 de 1990; [el parágrafo tercero del artículo 167 de la Ley 142 de 1994; el parágrafo tercero del artículo 32 de la Ley 143 de 1994]; el artículo 45 de la Ley 300 de 1996; artículos 15, 41, 44, 45, 46' (sanctioned 2023-05-19, Diario Oficial of 2023-05-19)).
- Derecho fundamental de petición (Ley 1755 de 2015), Reforma laboral (Ley 2466 de 2025), Plan Nacional de Desarrollo 2018-2022 (Ley 1955 de 2019): versions are rebuilt per article from the basedoc's earlier texts, inline amendment markers and Notas de Vigencia, as for the Constitution and the Código Civil. Where the source names an act but gives no text after it, the article reads '[La fuente no da el texto vigente en esta fecha.]' for that period.
- Constitutional Court rulings that struck text are laws of kind 'court', dated by the ruling's day as the source gives it; rulings that upheld text on a condition are court laws with a note and change no words.
- In-force days of amending acts: the act's own vigencia clause as its basedoc page gives it; otherwise the default rule (Ley 4 de 1913, art. 52: two months after promulgation). A day a provision's own note gives applies to that provision. The list below gives the day and the rule for each act.
- Dates come only from the act itself: the Diario Oficial day on the act's own page wins over a day the code's notes give; a note's Diario Oficial day is not given to an act named only inside another act's quoted title; and an act whose vigencia clause has a general rule and exceptions is dated by the rule that covers the changed provision (Ley 890 de 2004: arts. 7 to 13 immediately, the rest from 1 January 2005; Ley 2502 de 2025: art. 296 of the Código Penal one year after promulgation; Ley 2080 de 2021: from publication).
- The source's earlier texts often leave out the article's epigraph (its title); where the latest earlier text differs from today's only by that or by a change no note names, today's text is used from the latest named act's day. Articles where that happened: none.
- Derecho fundamental de petición (Ley 1755 de 2015): first version from 2015-06-30: Ley 1755 de 2015, art. 2: 'La presente ley rige a partir de la fecha de su promulgación y deroga las disposiciones que le sean contrarias.' (sanctioned 2015-06-30, Diario Oficial of 2015-06-30)
- Reforma laboral (Ley 2466 de 2025): first version from 2025-06-25: Ley 2466 de 2025, art. 70: 'La presente ley rige a partir de su promulgación y deroga o modifica todas las que le sean contrarias o incompatibles. Se deroga el literal b) del artículo 162 del Código Sustantivo del Trabajo. [...]' (sanctioned 2025-06-25, Diario Oficial of 2025-06-25)
- Plan Nacional de Desarrollo 2018-2022 (Ley 1955 de 2019): first version from 2019-05-25: Ley 1955 de 2019, art. 336: 'La presente ley rige a partir de su publicación y deroga todas las disposiciones que le sean contrarias. Los artículos de las Leyes 812 de 2003, 1151 de 2007, 1450 de 2011, y 1753 de 2015 no derogados expresamente en el siguiente Inciso o por otras leyes continuarán vigentes hasta que sean derogados o modificados por norma posterior. [...]' (sanctioned 2019-05-25, Diario Oficial of 2019-05-25)
- Plan Nacional de Desarrollo 2018-2022 (Ley 1955 de 2019): Ley 1955 de 2019: art. 130's earlier text is headed 'Texto original de la Ley 1955 de 2022'; it is the act's own original article (of 2019).
- Plan Nacional de Desarrollo 2018-2022 (Ley 1955 de 2019): Ley 1955 de 2019: art. 313's earlier text is headed 'Texto original de la Ley 1955 de 2020'; it is the act's own original article (of 2019).
- Derecho fundamental de petición (Ley 1755 de 2015) and the other acts of batch 38: an article an act repealed as a whole, whose last words the page still prints after the marker '<Artículo derogado por el artículo N de la Ley X>' and which has no earlier-text box, reads those words from the act's first day until the repeal, and is gone from it: ley_1955_2019 art-0163 (Ley 2294 de 2023).
- Reforma laboral (Ley 2466 de 2025): Ley 2466 de 2025, arts. 24 to 30 (trabajo en plataformas digitales de reparto): art. 67 says they 'comenzarán a regir doce (12) meses después de que el Gobierno nacional cumpla con las obligaciones de reglamentación e implementación allí señaladas', and each is printed under that marker. The source dates no such regulation, so their start day is unknown: they are in no version (open item: add them from their start day once a dated regulation fixes it).
- Plan Nacional de Desarrollo 2018-2022 (Ley 1955 de 2019): Ley 1955 de 2019: articles the page prints under '<Artículo INEXEQUIBLE ...>' markers, which the engine left out of every version. Their printed words are the text from the act's first day until the ruling takes effect: art. 18 until Sentencia C-484-20 (19 November 2020; the words of numeral 4 the page shows struck, 'y todos aquellos que inciden directa o indirectamente en la prestación de los servicios públicos domiciliarios', out from Sentencia C-464-20 of 28 October 2020, the ruling's day, although the marker says 'desde el 20 de octubre de 2020'); art. 314 until Sentencia C-147-21 (20 May 2021, immediate effect; C-464-20 had struck it from 1 January 2023); art. 193 until 20 June 2023 (Sentencia C-276-21 of 19 August 2021, effect deferred to that day).
- Plan Nacional de Desarrollo 2018-2022 (Ley 1955 de 2019): Ley 1955 de 2019, art. 244 (ingreso base de cotización de los independientes), struck by Sentencia C-068-20 (19 February 2020) with effect deferred to 21 July 2022: the incisos and the parágrafo the page prints from the act's first day; parágrafo 2 as Ley 2010 de 2019 (art. 139) added it (the earlier-text box) from 27 December 2019; its wording as Decreto 690 de 2020 corrected it (today's printed words) from that decree's day; the article out from 21 July 2022. The engine had only parágrafo 2, from 2019.

- Constitutional Court rulings that struck text are laws of kind 'court', dated by the ruling's day as the source gives it; rulings that upheld text on a condition are court laws with a note and change no words.

- Dates come only from the act itself: the Diario Oficial day on the act's own page wins over a day the code's notes give; a note's Diario Oficial day is not given to an act named only inside another act's quoted title; and an act whose vigencia clause has a general rule and exceptions is dated by the rule that covers the changed provision (Ley 890 de 2004: arts. 7 to 13 immediately, the rest from 1 January 2005; Ley 2502 de 2025: art. 296 of the Código Penal one year after promulgation; Ley 2080 de 2021: from publication).

- Sistema de Protección Social Integral para la Vejez, Invalidez y Muerte (Ley 2381 de 2024): Ley 2381 de 2024 (pension reform). Art. 95: 'la presente ley rige a partir de su sanción' (16 July 2024); art. 94 put the Sistema de Protección Social Integral para la Vejez in force on 1 July 2025; Auto A-841-25 of 27 June 2025 suspended that, except art. 12's parágrafo transitorio and art. 76 (both run from the law's issue: six months to choose an administrator, two years to change regime); Sentencia C-264-26 (25 August 2026) upheld the articles it reviewed, 'Entra en vigencia a partir del 1 de abril de 2027'. History: from 16 July 2024 the act holds art. 12's parágrafo transitorio (under the article's epigraph), art. 76 and arts. 94 and 95; on 1 April 2027 (the future branch) the rest of the text the page prints, except the provisions C-264-26 returned to the Cámara de Representantes, whose entry into force depends on a procedure not yet done: art. 63 inciso 2, art. 84 numeral 5, art. 92 inciso 1 and art. 93.

- In-force days of amending acts: the act's own vigencia clause as its basedoc page gives it; otherwise the default rule (Ley 4 de 1913, art. 52: two months after promulgation). A day a provision's own note gives applies to that provision. The list below gives the day and the rule for each act.
- Dates come only from the act itself: the Diario Oficial day on the act's own page wins over a day the code's notes give; a note's Diario Oficial day is not given to an act named only inside another act's quoted title; and an act whose vigencia clause has a general rule and exceptions is dated by the rule that covers the changed provision (Ley 890 de 2004: arts. 7 to 13 immediately, the rest from 1 January 2005; Ley 2502 de 2025: art. 296 of the Código Penal one year after promulgation; Ley 2080 de 2021: from publication).
- The source's earlier texts often leave out the article's epigraph (its title); where the latest earlier text differs from today's only by that or by a change no note names, today's text is used from the latest named act's day. Articles where that happened: none.

## What AI did and did not do

The scripts that read the sources and build this repository were written by Claude Code running Claude Opus 5.5 (`claude-opus-5-5`) under Lexifina's direction.

No model wrote, translated or corrected legal text. No model runs at build time. Every word of law in this repository comes from the official sources above.

The commit messages are written by `build.py` from the source data.

Model use at data-build time:
- None at data-build time: Lexifina's code is deterministic code, written with Claude Code (claude-opus-5-5); no model reads, writes or summarises legal text. The README was drafted with the same tool.
- None at data-build time: Lexifina's code is deterministic code, written with Claude Code (claude-opus-5-5); no model reads, writes or summarises legal text.
